{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Pz5b46t4KLbpVWQkt4dN24","type":"tool_result","content":"# @kitn.ai/ui\n\nFramework-agnostic web components for building AI chat interfaces — message threads, prompt inputs, streaming responses, markdown + code rendering, reasoning/tool panels, attachments, and a conversation sidebar. Drop them into any app: React, Vue, Angular, Svelte, or plain HTML.\n\nIt can be consumed two ways:\n\n1. **As framework-agnostic web components** *(primary)* — drop `<kai-chat>`, `<kai-conversations>`, and `<kai-prompt-input>` into any project (React, Vue, Angular, Svelte, plain HTML). Each is fully style-isolated via Shadow DOM, and the rendering runtime is bundled in, so the host needs nothing.\n2. **As native SolidJS components** — the kit is authored in SolidJS, so SolidJS apps can import the components directly for full compositional control. (This is a convenience for SolidJS users, not a requirement — the web components work everywhere.)\n\n## Highlights\n\n- **Composable components** across three layers: headless primitives → accessible UI primitives (built in-house, WCAG 2.1 AA — no third-party UI dependency) → AI feature components. `npm run verify:solid-coverage` prints the current element and component counts.\n- **Shadow-DOM web components** — zero CSS conflicts in any host. The host's styles can't leak in; the kit's Tailwind can't leak out.\n- **Load it your way** — register every element in one import, cherry-pick per-element with a bundler, or drop in a CDN autoloader that loads each on demand. Syntax highlighting loads lazily, per language, only when you render code.\n- **Tailwind v4** design tokens — rebrand by overriding `--color-*` custom properties.\n\n## Install\n\n```bash\nnpm install @kitn.ai/ui\n```\n\nSolidJS consumers also need `solid-js` (a peer dependency):\n\n```bash\nnpm install solid-js\n```\n\n## Quick start\n\n### Option A — Web components (any framework / plain HTML)\n\n`npm install` ships the built bundle, so there is nothing to compile. Import it once as a side effect and every element registers itself:\n\n```html\n<body style=\"height: 100vh; margin: 0;\">\n  <kai-chat style=\"display:block; height:100%;\"></kai-chat>\n\n  <script type=\"module\">\n    import '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';\n\n    // Registration is async (SSR-safe) — wait for the element to be defined\n    // before setting properties, or the upgrade clobbers them.\n    await customElements.whenDefined('kai-chat');\n\n    const chat = document.querySelector('kai-chat');\n\n    // Rich data is set as JS properties (not HTML attributes). A message's\n    // content is an ordered `parts` array.\n    chat.messages = [\n      { id: '1', role: 'assistant', parts: [{ type: 'text', text: 'Hello! How can I help?' }] },\n    ];\n\n    // Events are non-bubbling kai-* CustomEvents dispatched on the element\n    chat.addEventListener('kai-submit', (e) => {\n      console.log('user sent:', e.detail.value);\n    });\n  </script>\n</body>\n```\n\nThe element bundle is **ES-module only** and loads via `<script type=\"module\">` in every modern browser. See **[docs/web-components.md](https://github.com/kitn-ai/ui/blob/main/docs/web-components.md)** for the full element API (every property, event, and the `ChatMessage` schema).\n\n#### Or load from a CDN (no build, no npm)\n\nThe element bundle is a self-contained ES module — load it directly from [jsDelivr](https://www.jsdelivr.com/package/npm/@kitn.ai/ui) or [unpkg](https://unpkg.com/browse/@kitn.ai/ui/), no install or bundler required:\n\n```html\n<script type=\"module\">\n  import 'https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@kitn.ai/ui/dist/kai.es.js';\n  // …or unpkg: import 'https://unpkg.com/@kitn.ai/ui/dist/kai.es.js';\n</script>\n\n<kai-chat></kai-chat>\n```\n\n<!-- x-release-please-start-version -->\nThe URLs above track the **latest** release — handy for trying things out. **For production, pin an exact version** (e.g. `@kitn.ai/ui@0.26.0/dist/kai.es.js`): pinned URLs are immutable and cached far more aggressively, and — since this package is pre-1.0 — pinning shields you from breaking changes in a future minor release.\n<!-- x-release-please-end -->\n\n> **Pin `0.25.0` or newer.** Every version from `0.14.1` through `0.24.0` is covered by a [critical security advisory](https://github.com/kitn-ai/ui/security/advisories) and is deprecated on npm. `npm install` warns you about a deprecated version; **a CDN fetch does not**, so an old pinned URL in a page keeps serving the vulnerable bundle silently.\n\nSolidJS and the kit's CSS are bundled in, and the lazy code-highlighting chunks load from the same CDN on demand. To override design tokens, also include `theme.css`:\n\n```html\n<link rel=\"stylesheet\" href=\"https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@kitn.ai/ui/theme.css\">\n```\n\n### Option B — SolidJS components\n\n```tsx\nimport {\n  ChatConfig, ChatContainer, ChatContainerContent,\n  Message, MessageContent,\n  PromptInput, PromptInputTextarea, PromptInputActions,\n} from '@kitn.ai/ui';\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/theme.css';\n\nfunction App() {\n  const [input, setInput] = createSignal('');\n  return (\n    <ChatConfig proseSize=\"sm\">\n      <ChatContainer class=\"h-full\">\n        <ChatContainerContent class=\"space-y-4 p-4\">\n          <Message>\n            <MessageContent markdown>{`## Hi\\n\\nAsk me anything.`}</MessageContent>\n          </Message>\n        </ChatContainerContent>\n      </ChatContainer>\n      <PromptInput value={input()} onValueChange={setInput} onSubmit={() => setInput('')}>\n        <PromptInputTextarea placeholder=\"Ask anything...\" />\n        <PromptInputActions>{/* your buttons */}</PromptInputActions>\n      </PromptInput>\n    </ChatConfig>\n  );\n}\n```\n\nThe SolidJS entry (`.`) ships compiled — your bundler tree-shakes it to just the components you import.\n\n## Integrations\n\nThe components are deliberately **transport-agnostic**: `<kai-chat>` just renders the `messages` array you give it and emits a `submit` event when the user sends. You own the request, the streaming, and any extras like text-to-speech. The patterns below use the web component, but the same wiring applies to the SolidJS API.\n\n### Streaming responses from OpenRouter\n\n[OpenRouter](https://openrouter.ai) exposes an OpenAI-compatible streaming API (Server-Sent Events). On `submit`, append the user message + an empty assistant message, then grow the assistant message as tokens arrive.\n\n> **Security:** never ship your API key to the browser. In production, point `fetch` at your own backend endpoint that proxies to OpenRouter and injects the key. The parsing below is identical either way.\n\n```html\n<kai-chat id=\"chat\" style=\"display:block; height:100vh;\"></kai-chat>\n\n<script type=\"module\">\n  import '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';\n  import { createAssistantStream } from '@kitn.ai/ui/state';\n  import { readOpenAIStream, toOpenAIMessages } from '@kitn.ai/ui/wire';\n\n  // Registration is async (SSR-safe) — wait for the element before using it.\n  await customElements.whenDefined('kai-chat');\n\n  const chat = document.getElementById('chat');\n  chat.messages = [];\n\n  chat.addEventListener('kai-submit', async (e) => {\n    const text = e.detail.value.trim();\n    if (!text) return;\n\n    // 1. Show the user message immediately\n    const history = [...chat.messages,\n      { id: crypto.randomUUID(), role: 'user', parts: [{ type: 'text', text }] }];\n    chat.messages = history;\n    chat.value = '';        // clear the input\n    chat.loading = true;\n\n    // 2. Open the assistant message. createAssistantStream appends it and folds\n    //    each delta onto the right part, reassigning `messages` every time.\n    const stream = createAssistantStream((update) => {\n      chat.messages = update(chat.messages);\n    });\n\n    try {\n      // In production, replace this URL with your own proxy endpoint.\n      const res = await fetch('https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/chat/completions', {\n        method: 'POST',\n        headers: {\n          'Authorization': `Bearer ${OPENROUTER_API_KEY}`, // server-side in production!\n          'Content-Type': 'application/json',\n        },\n        body: JSON.stringify({\n          model: 'anthropic/claude-sonnet-4',\n          stream: true,\n          // The encoder turns the thread into the OpenAI `messages` array,\n          // keeping tool calls and their results in the order the model needs.\n          messages: toOpenAIMessages(history),\n        }),\n      });\n\n      // Parses the whole stream: keep-alive comments, multi-line frames,\n      // codepoints split across a socket boundary, tool calls, reasoning.\n      await readOpenAIStream(res, stream);\n    } catch (err) {\n      // A non-ok response throws WireError before a single chunk is read, so put\n      // the reason in the bubble rather than leaving an empty one.\n      stream.appendText('Request failed: ' + err.message);\n    } finally {\n      stream.done();\n      chat.loading = false;\n    }\n  });\n</script>\n```\n\nKey point, and it is not only a streaming rule: `chat.messages` has to be reassigned with a **new array**, and any message you changed has to be a **new object**. The array reference is what tells the element something changed — reassigning the same array is a no-op even if you swapped a message inside it. The new message object is what makes the change visible, because the list keys its rows by item identity. Adds, removes and reorders need only the fresh array; editing an existing message needs both, and mutating one in place won't update the view even inside a fresh array. `createAssistantStream` satisfies both halves for you; if you drive `messages` by hand — editing a message, renaming a conversation — you own it.\n\n`@kitn.ai/ui/wire` also ships `readAnthropicStream`, the helpers for a multi-round tool loop, and a seam for a custom wire format. See the [wire adapter recipe](https://ui.kitn.ai/guides/recipes/wire-adapter/).\n\n### Text-to-speech (TTS)\n\n#### Option 1 — Browser-native (zero dependencies)\n\nThe Web Speech API speaks text with no network call. `readOpenAIStream` resolves with the finished turn, so speak `turn.text` in the example above:\n\n```js\nconst turn = await readOpenAIStream(res, stream);\nspeak(turn.text);\n\nfunction speak(text) {\n  if (!('speechSynthesis' in window)) return;\n  const utter = new SpeechSynthesisUtterance(text);\n  utter.lang = 'en-US';\n  utter.rate = 1;\n  speechSynthesis.cancel();   // stop anything already playing\n  speechSynthesis.speak(utter);\n}\n```\n\nTo speak *as it streams*, flush complete sentences instead of waiting for the end:\n\n```js\nlet spokenUpTo = 0;\nfunction speakIncremental(fullText) {\n  const lastBreak = fullText.lastIndexOf('. ', fullText.length);\n  if (lastBreak > spokenUpTo) {\n    speak(fullText.slice(spokenUpTo, lastBreak + 1));\n    spokenUpTo = lastBreak + 1;\n  }\n}\n\n// A sink is a plain object, so wrap it to see the text as it arrives. Everything\n// else passes through untouched.\nlet sofar = '';\nconst speaking = {\n  ...stream,\n  appendText(delta) {\n    sofar += delta;\n    speakIncremental(sofar);\n    return stream.appendText(delta);\n  },\n};\nawait readOpenAIStream(res, speaking);\n```\n\n#### Option 2 — Cloud TTS (OpenAI, ElevenLabs, …)\n\nFor higher-quality voices, have your backend call a TTS API and return audio, then play it. Keep the provider key on the server.\n\n```js\nasync function speakCloud(text) {\n  const res = await fetch('/api/tts', {\n    method: 'POST',\n    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },\n    body: JSON.stringify({ text, voice: 'alloy' }),\n  });\n  const audio = new Audio(URL.createObjectURL(await res.blob()));\n  audio.play();\n}\n```\n\n```js\n// Example backend (Node) — /api/tts proxying OpenAI's speech endpoint\n// const r = await openai.audio.speech.create({ model: 'gpt-4o-mini-tts', voice, input: text });\n// res.setHeader('Content-Type', 'audio/mpeg');\n// Readable.fromWeb(r.body).pipe(res);\n```\n\nYou can trigger either option from the streaming completion (auto-read replies) or from a button you render alongside each message.\n\n> **Speech-to-text** (the other direction) is already built in — the kit ships a `VoiceInput` component for capturing microphone input. See Storybook (`npm run dev`).\n\n## State helpers & hooks\n\n`@kitn.ai/ui/state` ships immutable helpers (`appendMessage`, `upsertMessage`, `updateMessage`, `removeMessage`, `appendText`, `textMessage`), the part-level folds they build on (`appendTextPart`, `appendReasoningPart`, `upsertToolPart`, `upsertCardPart`), and a streaming handle (`createAssistantStream`) so you don't hand-roll array mutations. React apps get `useKaiChat` (from `@kitn.ai/ui/react`); SolidJS apps get `createKaiChat` (from `@kitn.ai/ui`). Both return a `bind` object to spread directly onto the element:\n\n```tsx\nimport { Chat, useKaiChat } from '@kitn.ai/ui/react';\n\nfunction App() {\n  const chat = useKaiChat({\n    async onSubmit({ value }) {\n      chat.append({ id: crypto.randomUUID(), role: 'user', parts: [{ type: 'text', text: value }] });\n      const s = chat.streamAssistant();\n      for await (const part of backend(value)) s.appendText(part);\n      s.done();\n    },\n  });\n  return <Chat {...chat.bind} />;\n}\n```\n\nEach hook instance owns its own message list — instantiate multiple times for multiple independent chats on one page. Full API: [ui.kitn.ai/guides/state-and-hooks/](https://ui.kitn.ai/guides/state-and-hooks/).\n\n## Code highlighting (optional, on-demand)\n\nSyntax highlighting uses [Shiki](https://shiki.style) and is wired to be as light as possible:\n\n- Nothing loads until a fenced code block actually renders.\n- Only the core, the **JavaScript regex engine (no WASM)**, the one theme, and the one language grammar needed are fetched — each a small lazy chunk.\n- Default languages: `javascript`/`js`, `typescript`/`ts`, `tsx`, `json`, `bash`/`sh`. Add more or turn it off:\n\n```js\nimport { configureCodeHighlighting } from '@kitn.ai/ui/elements'; // or '@kitn.ai/ui'\n\nconfigureCodeHighlighting({\n  languages: { python: () => import('@shikijs/langs/python') },\n});\n\n// or disable entirely — no Shiki ever loads:\nconfigureCodeHighlighting({ enabled: false });\n```\n\nPer element: `<kai-chat codeHighlight={false}>` renders code as plain text.\n\n## Theming\n\nVisual appearance is driven by `--color-*` CSS custom properties in `theme.css`. Because inherited CSS pierces the Shadow DOM boundary, overriding tokens on `:root` rebrands the components — even the web-component ones:\n\n```css\n:root {\n  --color-background: #0f0f0f;\n  --color-primary: #7c3aed;\n  --color-muted: #1e1e1e;\n}\n```\n\nFor SolidJS usage, import `@kitn.ai/ui/theme.css` once. For web components the kit's CSS is injected into each shadow root automatically; only `theme.css` (design tokens) is optional to include.\n\n## For AI agents / LLMs\n\nThe package ships [llmstxt.org](https://llmstxt.org)-style files so coding agents (Claude Code, Copilot, Cursor, Codex) can wire up the components correctly:\n\n- **[`llms.txt`](./llms.txt)** — dense orientation: install, the property-vs-attribute rule, the two-layer architecture, theming, and framework wiring.\n- **[`llms-full.txt`](./llms-full.txt)** — the above plus a generated props/events reference for every `kai-*` element, a streaming recipe, and a build runbook.\n\nBoth are auto-generated from `dist/custom-elements.json` during `npm run build` (so they never drift) and are published in the npm package — find them at `node_modules/@kitn.ai/ui/llms.txt` after install.\n\n> **#1 thing agents get wrong:** array/object data (`messages`, `models`, `context`, …) must be set as **JS properties**, not HTML attributes. Only scalars (`placeholder`, `loading`, `theme`) work as attributes.\n\n## Development\n\n```bash\nnpm install          # install dependencies\nnpm run dev          # Storybook dev server at http://localhost:6006 (component playground)\nnpm test             # run the test suite (Vitest: jsdom unit tests + Storybook browser tests)\nnpm run build        # build the web-component bundle into dist/\nnpm run build-storybook   # static Storybook build\n```\n\nStorybook is the primary way to explore and develop components in isolation.\n\n### Testing the consumer experience (Claude Code)\n\n`npm test` covers the kit's internals. To test what a **consumer of the published package** experiences — building real chat apps across every framework (React, Next.js, Vue, Svelte, HTML, TanStack Start), archetype, integration, and backend, against a *local* build of the package — this repo ships a project-local [Claude Code](https://code.claude.com) skill that auto-loads when you open Claude Code anywhere in the repo (no setup):\n\n- Run **`/consumer-regression`** — modes: `smoke` (one parallel pass, report only) and `regression` (the full build → triage → fix → re-verify loop). It fans out parallel agents and varies model tiers to measure how cheap a model can drive the tooling.\n- Source + full methodology: **[`.claude/skills/consumer-regression/SKILL.md`](.claude/skills/consumer-regression/SKILL.md)** (and `recipes.md` beside it). The agents it deploys live in [`.claude/agents/`](.claude/agents/) (`consumer-probe`, `regression-triage`).\n\nThis catches packaging / exports / SSR / scaffold-output bugs that the unit suite can't. See [`.claude/README.md`](.claude/README.md) for the full index of project-local Claude Code tooling.\n\n### Project structure\n\n```\nsrc/\n  primitives/    Headless logic hooks + ChatConfig + on-demand highlighter\n  ui/            Accessible UI primitives (Button, Dropdown, Tooltip, HoverCard, … built in-house, no third-party UI deps)\n  components/    AI feature components (Message, PromptInput, Markdown, Tool, …)\n  elements/      Web-component facades + defineWebComponent wrapper + Vite lib entry\n  stories/       Composed example stories (full chat app, layouts)\ntheme.css        Design tokens (--color-*), animations, markdown styles\ndocs/\n  web-components.md   Full web-component API reference\n```\n\nThe web-component layer wraps a few coarse facades over the SolidJS components; the SolidJS API stays the source of truth and is unchanged by it.\n\n## Examples\n\nA set of runnable examples and a hosted component playground are included in the repo. See [`examples/README.md`](examples/README.md) for full details.\n\n### Composable showcase\n\nThe composable showcase demonstrates every individual element in one page. Build the package first, then serve from the repo root:\n\n```bash\nnpm run build     # produces dist/kai.es.js\nnpm run examples  # static server at http://localhost:8000\n```\n\nThen open: **http://localhost:8000/examples/composable/index.html**\n\n### Storybook\n\nStorybook is the primary component playground for development:\n\n```bash\nnpm run dev    # dev server at http://localhost:6006\n```\n\nThe published docs are deployed to GitHub Pages:\n**https://ui.kitn.ai/**\n\n### Framework example apps\n\nThe `examples/react`, `examples/solid`, `examples/angular`, and `examples/vue` directories are full Vite apps — install their dependencies and run the local dev server:\n\n```bash\ncd examples/react && npm install && npm run dev\n# or\ncd examples/solid && npm install && npm run dev\n# or\ncd examples/angular && npm install && npm run dev\n# or\ncd examples/vue && npm install && npm run dev\n```\n\n- `examples/react` — uses the generated React wrappers from `@kitn.ai/ui/react`\n- `examples/solid` — uses the raw SolidJS component API\n- `examples/angular` — uses the web components natively via Angular's `[prop]` / `(event)` bindings with `CUSTOM_ELEMENTS_SCHEMA` (no wrappers needed)\n- `examples/vue` — uses the web components natively via Vue's `:prop.prop` modifier and `@event` bindings; `isCustomElement` in `vite.config.ts` prevents Vue treating `kai-*` tags as Vue components\n\n### Docs and reference\n\n- **[docs/web-components.md](https://github.com/kitn-ai/ui/blob/main/docs/web-components.md)** — full element API: every property, event, and the `ChatMessage` schema.\n- **[llms.txt](llms.txt)** / **[llms-full.txt](llms-full.txt)** — dense machine-readable references for AI coding agents.\n\n## Loading\n\nThree ways to load the elements — register all (the simple default), cherry-pick per-element, or a CDN autoloader:\n\n| How you load | What it does |\n|---|---|\n| `import '@kitn.ai/ui/elements'` | registers every element — the simple default |\n| `import '@kitn.ai/ui/elements/chat'` | one element, bundler tree-shakes |\n| `import '@kitn.ai/ui/autoloader'` (opt-in **DOM autoloader**) | loads each element's module **on demand** as `<kai-*>` appears |\n\nThe autoloader watches the DOM (initial scan + `MutationObserver`) and dynamically imports only the\nelements actually present — so a page with just `<kai-chat>` never downloads the others. It's additive:\nthe register-all bundle stays the default. (Direct per-element imports and the autoloader are client-side;\nSSR apps use the register-all `@kitn.ai/ui/elements` or render client-only.)\n\nCode highlighting (Shiki) is always lazy — loaded per-language on first code block, with no WASM, and never\nat all if you don't render code. How can I help?' }] },\n    ];\n\n    // Events are non-bubbling kai-* CustomEvents dispatched on the element\n    chat.addEventListener('kai-submit', (e) => {\n      console.log('user sent:', e.detail.value);\n    });\n  </script>\n</body>\n```\n\nThe element bundle is **ES-module only** and loads via `<script type=\"module\">` in every modern browser. See **[docs/web-components.md](https://github.com/kitn-ai/ui/blob/main/docs/web-components.md)** for the full element API (every property, event, and the `ChatMessage` schema).\n\n#### Or load from a CDN (no build, no npm)\n\nThe element bundle is a self-contained ES module — load it directly from [jsDelivr](https://www.jsdelivr.com/package/npm/@kitn.ai/ui) or [unpkg](https://unpkg.com/browse/@kitn.ai/ui/), no install or bundler required:\n\n```html\n<script type=\"module\">\n  import 'https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@kitn.ai/ui/dist/kai.es.js';\n  // …or unpkg: import 'https://unpkg.com/@kitn.ai/ui/dist/kai.es.js';\n</script>\n\n<kai-chat></kai-chat>\n```\n\n<!-- x-release-please-start-version -->\nThe URLs above track the **latest** release — handy for trying things out. **For production, pin an exact version** (e.g. `@kitn.ai/ui@0.26.0/dist/kai.es.js`): pinned URLs are immutable and cached far more aggressively, and — since this package is pre-1.0 — pinning shields you from breaking changes in a future minor release.\n<!-- x-release-please-end -->\n\n> **Pin `0.25.0` or newer.** Every version from `0.14.1` through `0.24.0` is covered by a [critical security advisory](https://github.com/kitn-ai/ui/security/advisories) and is deprecated on npm. `npm install` warns you about a deprecated version; **a CDN fetch does not**, so an old pinned URL in a page keeps serving the vulnerable bundle silently.\n\nSolidJS and the kit's CSS are bundled in, and the lazy code-highlighting chunks load from the same CDN on demand. To override design tokens, also include `theme.css`:\n\n```html\n<link rel=\"stylesheet\" href=\"https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@kitn.ai/ui/theme.css\">\n```\n\n### Option B — SolidJS components\n\n```tsx\nimport {\n  ChatConfig, ChatContainer, ChatContainerContent,\n  Message, MessageContent,\n  PromptInput, PromptInputTextarea, PromptInputActions,\n} from '@kitn.ai/ui';\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/theme.css';\n\nfunction App() {\n  const [input, setInput] = createSignal('');\n  return (\n    <ChatConfig proseSize=\"sm\">\n      <ChatContainer class=\"h-full\">\n        <ChatContainerContent class=\"space-y-4 p-4\">\n          <Message>\n            <MessageContent markdown>{`## Hi\\n\\nAsk me anything.`}</MessageContent>\n          </Message>\n        </ChatContainerContent>\n      </ChatContainer>\n      <PromptInput value={input()} onValueChange={setInput} onSubmit={() => setInput('')}>\n        <PromptInputTextarea placeholder=\"Ask anything...\" />\n        <PromptInputActions>{/* your buttons */}</PromptInputActions>\n      </PromptInput>\n    </ChatConfig>\n  );\n}\n```\n\nThe SolidJS entry (`.`) ships compiled — your bundler tree-shakes it to just the components you import.\n\n## Integrations\n\nThe components are deliberately **transport-agnostic**: `<kai-chat>` just renders the `messages` array you give it and emits a `submit` event when the user sends. You own the request, the streaming, and any extras like text-to-speech. The patterns below use the web component, but the same wiring applies to the SolidJS API.\n\n### Streaming responses from OpenRouter\n\n[OpenRouter](https://openrouter.ai) exposes an OpenAI-compatible streaming API (Server-Sent Events). On `submit`, append the user message + an empty assistant message, then grow the assistant message as tokens arrive.\n\n> **Security:** never ship your API key to the browser. In production, point `fetch` at your own backend endpoint that proxies to OpenRouter and injects the key. The parsing below is identical either way.\n\n```html\n<kai-chat id=\"chat\" style=\"display:block; height:100vh;\"></kai-chat>\n\n<script type=\"module\">\n  import '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';\n  import { createAssistantStream } from '@kitn.ai/ui/state';\n  import { readOpenAIStream, toOpenAIMessages } from '@kitn.ai/ui/wire';\n\n  // Registration is async (SSR-safe) — wait for the element before using it.\n  await customElements.whenDefined('kai-chat');\n\n  const chat = document.getElementById('chat');\n  chat.messages = [];\n\n  chat.addEventListener('kai-submit', async (e) => {\n    const text = e.detail.value.trim();\n    if (!text) return;\n\n    // 1. Show the user message immediately\n    const history = [...chat.messages,\n      { id: crypto.randomUUID(), role: 'user', parts: [{ type: 'text', text }] }];\n    chat.messages = history;\n    chat.value = '';        // clear the input\n    chat.loading = true;\n\n    // 2. Open the assistant message. createAssistantStream appends it and folds\n    //    each delta onto the right part, reassigning `messages` every time.\n    const stream = createAssistantStream((update) => {\n      chat.messages = update(chat.messages);\n    });\n\n    try {\n      // In production, replace this URL with your own proxy endpoint.\n      const res = await fetch('https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/chat/completions', {\n        method: 'POST',\n        headers: {\n          'Authorization': `Bearer ${OPENROUTER_API_KEY}`, // server-side in production!\n          'Content-Type': 'application/json',\n        },\n        body: JSON.stringify({\n          model: 'anthropic/claude-sonnet-4',\n          stream: true,\n          // The encoder turns the thread into the OpenAI `messages` array,\n          // keeping tool calls and their results in the order the model needs.\n          messages: toOpenAIMessages(history),\n        }),\n      });\n\n      // Parses the whole stream: keep-alive comments, multi-line frames,\n      // codepoints split across a socket boundary, tool calls, reasoning.\n      await readOpenAIStream(res, stream);\n    } catch (err) {\n      // A non-ok response throws WireError before a single chunk is read, so put\n      // the reason in the bubble rather than leaving an empty one.\n      stream.appendText('Request failed: ' + err.message);\n    } finally {\n      stream.done();\n      chat.loading = false;\n    }\n  });\n</script>\n```\n\nKey point, and it is not only a streaming rule: `chat.messages` has to be reassigned with a **new array**, and any message you changed has to be a **new object**. The array reference is what tells the element something changed — reassigning the same array is a no-op even if you swapped a message inside it. The new message object is what makes the change visible, because the list keys its rows by item identity. Adds, removes and reorders need only the fresh array; editing an existing message needs both, and mutating one in place won't update the view even inside a fresh array. `createAssistantStream` satisfies both halves for you; if you drive `messages` by hand — editing a message, renaming a conversation — you own it.\n\n`@kitn.ai/ui/wire` also ships `readAnthropicStream`, the helpers for a multi-round tool loop, and a seam for a custom wire format. See the [wire adapter recipe](https://ui.kitn.ai/guides/recipes/wire-adapter/).\n\n### Text-to-speech (TTS)\n\n#### Option 1 — Browser-native (zero dependencies)\n\nThe Web Speech API speaks text with no network call. `readOpenAIStream` resolves with the finished turn, so speak `turn.text` in the example above:\n\n```js\nconst turn = await readOpenAIStream(res, stream);\nspeak(turn.text);\n\nfunction speak(text) {\n  if (!('speechSynthesis' in window)) return;\n  const utter = new SpeechSynthesisUtterance(text);\n  utter.lang = 'en-US';\n  utter.rate = 1;\n  speechSynthesis.cancel();   // stop anything already playing\n  speechSynthesis.speak(utter);\n}\n```\n\nTo speak *as it streams*, flush complete sentences instead of waiting for the end:\n\n```js\nlet spokenUpTo = 0;\nfunction speakIncremental(fullText) {\n  const lastBreak = fullText.lastIndexOf('. ', fullText.length);\n  if (lastBreak > spokenUpTo) {\n    speak(fullText.slice(spokenUpTo, lastBreak + 1));\n    spokenUpTo = lastBreak + 1;\n  }\n}\n\n// A sink is a plain object, so wrap it to see the text as it arrives. Everything\n// else passes through untouched.\nlet sofar = '';\nconst speaking = {\n  ...stream,\n  appendText(delta) {\n    sofar += delta;\n    speakIncremental(sofar);\n    return stream.appendText(delta);\n  },\n};\nawait readOpenAIStream(res, speaking);\n```\n\n#### Option 2 — Cloud TTS (OpenAI, ElevenLabs, …)\n\nFor higher-quality voices, have your backend call a TTS API and return audio, then play it. Keep the provider key on the server.\n\n```js\nasync function speakCloud(text) {\n  const res = await fetch('/api/tts', {\n    method: 'POST',\n    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },\n    body: JSON.stringify({ text, voice: 'alloy' }),\n  });\n  const audio = new Audio(URL.createObjectURL(await res.blob()));\n  audio.play();\n}\n```\n\n```js\n// Example backend (Node) — /api/tts proxying OpenAI's speech endpoint\n// const r = await openai.audio.speech.create({ model: 'gpt-4o-mini-tts', voice, input: text });\n// res.setHeader('Content-Type', 'audio/mpeg');\n// Readable.fromWeb(r.body).pipe(res);\n```\n\nYou can trigger either option from the streaming completion (auto-read replies) or from a button you render alongside each message.\n\n> **Speech-to-text** (the other direction) is already built in — the kit ships a `VoiceInput` component for capturing microphone input. See Storybook (`npm run dev`).\n\n## State helpers & hooks\n\n`@kitn.ai/ui/state` ships immutable helpers (`appendMessage`, `upsertMessage`, `updateMessage`, `removeMessage`, `appendText`, `textMessage`), the part-level folds they build on (`appendTextPart`, `appendReasoningPart`, `upsertToolPart`, `upsertCardPart`), and a streaming handle (`createAssistantStream`) so you don't hand-roll array mutations. React apps get `useKaiChat` (from `@kitn.ai/ui/react`); SolidJS apps get `createKaiChat` (from `@kitn.ai/ui`). Both return a `bind` object to spread directly onto the element:\n\n```tsx\nimport { Chat, useKaiChat } from '@kitn.ai/ui/react';\n\nfunction App() {\n  const chat = useKaiChat({\n    async onSubmit({ value }) {\n      chat.append({ id: crypto.randomUUID(), role: 'user', parts: [{ type: 'text', text: value }] });\n      const s = chat.streamAssistant();\n      for await (const part of backend(value)) s.appendText(part);\n      s.done();\n    },\n  });\n  return <Chat {...chat.bind} />;\n}\n```\n\nEach hook instance owns its own message list — instantiate multiple times for multiple independent chats on one page. Full API: [ui.kitn.ai/guides/state-and-hooks/](https://ui.kitn.ai/guides/state-and-hooks/).\n\n## Code highlighting (optional, on-demand)\n\nSyntax highlighting uses [Shiki](https://shiki.style) and is wired to be as light as possible:\n\n- Nothing loads until a fenced code block actually renders.\n- Only the core, the **JavaScript regex engine (no WASM)**, the one theme, and the one language grammar needed are fetched — each a small lazy chunk.\n- Default languages: `javascript`/`js`, `typescript`/`ts`, `tsx`, `json`, `bash`/`sh`. Add more or turn it off:\n\n```js\nimport { configureCodeHighlighting } from '@kitn.ai/ui/elements'; // or '@kitn.ai/ui'\n\nconfigureCodeHighlighting({\n  languages: { python: () => import('@shikijs/langs/python') },\n});\n\n// or disable entirely — no Shiki ever loads:\nconfigureCodeHighlighting({ enabled: false });\n```\n\nPer element: `<kai-chat codeHighlight={false}>` renders code as plain text.\n\n## Theming\n\nVisual appearance is driven by `--color-*` CSS custom properties in `theme.css`. Because inherited CSS pierces the Shadow DOM boundary, overriding tokens on `:root` rebrands the components — even the web-component ones:\n\n```css\n:root {\n  --color-background: #0f0f0f;\n  --color-primary: #7c3aed;\n  --color-muted: #1e1e1e;\n}\n```\n\nFor SolidJS usage, import `@kitn.ai/ui/theme.css` once. For web components the kit's CSS is injected into each shadow root automatically; only `theme.css` (design tokens) is optional to include.\n\n## For AI agents / LLMs\n\nThe package ships [llmstxt.org](https://llmstxt.org)-style files so coding agents (Claude Code, Copilot, Cursor, Codex) can wire up the components correctly:\n\n- **[`llms.txt`](./llms.txt)** — dense orientation: install, the property-vs-attribute rule, the two-layer architecture, theming, and framework wiring.\n- **[`llms-full.txt`](./llms-full.txt)** — the above plus a generated props/events reference for every `kai-*` element, a streaming recipe, and a build runbook.\n\nBoth are auto-generated from `dist/custom-elements.json` during `npm run build` (so they never drift) and are published in the npm package — find them at `node_modules/@kitn.ai/ui/llms.txt` after install.\n\n> **#1 thing agents get wrong:** array/object data (`messages`, `models`, `context`, …) must be set as **JS properties**, not HTML attributes. Only scalars (`placeholder`, `loading`, `theme`) work as attributes.\n\n## Development\n\n```bash\nnpm install          # install dependencies\nnpm run dev          # Storybook dev server at http://localhost:6006 (component playground)\nnpm test             # run the test suite (Vitest: jsdom unit tests + Storybook browser tests)\nnpm run build        # build the web-component bundle into dist/\nnpm run build-storybook   # static Storybook build\n```\n\nStorybook is the primary way to explore and develop components in isolation.\n\n### Testing the consumer experience (Claude Code)\n\n`npm test` covers the kit's internals. To test what a **consumer of the published package** experiences — building real chat apps across every framework (React, Next.js, Vue, Svelte, HTML, TanStack Start), archetype, integration, and backend, against a *local* build of the package — this repo ships a project-local [Claude Code](https://code.claude.com) skill that auto-loads when you open Claude Code anywhere in the repo (no setup):\n\n- Run **`/consumer-regression`** — modes: `smoke` (one parallel pass, report only) and `regression` (the full build → triage → fix → re-verify loop). It fans out parallel agents and varies model tiers to measure how cheap a model can drive the tooling.\n- Source + full methodology: **[`.claude/skills/consumer-regression/SKILL.md`](.claude/skills/consumer-regression/SKILL.md)** (and `recipes.md` beside it). The agents it deploys live in [`.claude/agents/`](.claude/agents/) (`consumer-probe`, `regression-triage`).\n\nThis catches packaging / exports / SSR / scaffold-output bugs that the unit suite can't. See [`.claude/README.md`](.claude/README.md) for the full index of project-local Claude Code tooling.\n\n### Project structure\n\n```\nsrc/\n  primitives/    Headless logic hooks + ChatConfig + on-demand highlighter\n  ui/            Accessible UI primitives (Button, Dropdown, Tooltip, HoverCard, … built in-house, no third-party UI deps)\n  components/    AI feature components (Message, PromptInput, Markdown, Tool, …)\n  elements/      Web-component facades + defineWebComponent wrapper + Vite lib entry\n  stories/       Composed example stories (full chat app, layouts)\ntheme.css        Design tokens (--color-*), animations, markdown styles\ndocs/\n  web-components.md   Full web-component API reference\n```\n\nThe web-component layer wraps a few coarse facades over the SolidJS components; the SolidJS API stays the source of truth and is unchanged by it.\n\n## Examples\n\nA set of runnable examples and a hosted component playground are included in the repo. See [`examples/README.md`](examples/README.md) for full details.\n\n### Composable showcase\n\nThe composable showcase demonstrates every individual element in one page. Build the package first, then serve from the repo root:\n\n```bash\nnpm run build     # produces dist/kai.es.js\nnpm run examples  # static server at http://localhost:8000\n```\n\nThen open: **http://localhost:8000/examples/composable/index.html**\n\n### Storybook\n\nStorybook is the primary component playground for development:\n\n```bash\nnpm run dev    # dev server at http://localhost:6006\n```\n\nThe published docs are deployed to GitHub Pages:\n**https://ui.kitn.ai/**\n\n### Framework example apps\n\nThe `examples/react`, `examples/solid`, `examples/angular`, and `examples/vue` directories are full Vite apps — install their dependencies and run the local dev server:\n\n```bash\ncd examples/react && npm install && npm run dev\n# or\ncd examples/solid && npm install && npm run dev\n# or\ncd examples/angular && npm install && npm run dev\n# or\ncd examples/vue && npm install && npm run dev\n```\n\n- `examples/react` — uses the generated React wrappers from `@kitn.ai/ui/react`\n- `examples/solid` — uses the raw SolidJS component API\n- `examples/angular` — uses the web components natively via Angular's `[prop]` / `(event)` bindings with `CUSTOM_ELEMENTS_SCHEMA` (no wrappers needed)\n- `examples/vue` — uses the web components natively via Vue's `:prop.prop` modifier and `@event` bindings; `isCustomElement` in `vite.config.ts` prevents Vue treating `kai-*` tags as Vue components\n\n### Docs and reference\n\n- **[docs/web-components.md](https://github.com/kitn-ai/ui/blob/main/docs/web-components.md)** — full element API: every property, event, and the `ChatMessage` schema.\n- **[llms.txt](llms.txt)** / **[llms-full.txt](llms-full.txt)** — dense machine-readable references for AI coding agents.\n\n## Loading\n\nThree ways to load the elements — register all (the simple default), cherry-pick per-element, or a CDN autoloader:\n\n| How you load | What it does |\n|---|---|\n| `import '@kitn.ai/ui/elements'` | registers every element — the simple default |\n| `import '@kitn.ai/ui/elements/chat'` | one element, bundler tree-shakes |\n| `import '@kitn.ai/ui/autoloader'` (opt-in **DOM autoloader**) | loads each element's module **on demand** as `<kai-*>` appears |\n\nThe autoloader watches the DOM (initial scan + `MutationObserver`) and dynamically imports only the\nelements actually present — so a page with just `<kai-chat>` never downloads the others. It's additive:\nthe register-all bundle stays the default. (Direct per-element imports and the autoloader are client-side;\nSSR apps use the register-all `@kitn.ai/ui/elements` or render client-only.)\n\nCode highlighting (Shiki) is always lazy — loaded per-language on first code block, with no WASM, and never\nat all if you don't render code. The build is ES-module only (a UMD/IIFE build can't code-split).","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_017cRMqM1D2TKF7ygnHz8YdZ","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"## <kai-thread>\n\n### Getting the element\nRegister it before you use it. If you skip registering it the element never upgrades: nothing renders, **no error and no warning is logged**, a property you set assigns and reads back correctly, and `customElements.whenDefined('kai-thread')` never resolves.\n\n```ts\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements/thread'; // or just this one\n```\n\nTypeScript: `import type { KaiThreadElement } from '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';` — the element interface ships with the package; do not hand-roll a structural type.\n\n### AI/UI contract\n`kai-*` elements accept **array and object data as JavaScript properties** (set in JavaScript via `el.property = value`, not as HTML attributes). Events are native CustomEvents — listen with `el.addEventListener(\"event-name\", handler)` and read `event.detail` for the payload.\n\n### Card contract\n`<kai-thread>` renders generative-UI cards inside the thread. It is a HOST: it draws whatever envelopes arrive, and carries the two props that say what a card may be.\n\n- **`cardTypes`** — `Record<envelope type, custom element tag>`. What DRAWS a card. Merged over the built-ins, so your entry overrides ours and the other 6 still render.\n- **`cardSchemas`** — `Record<envelope type, JSON Schema>`. What a VALID card looks like. This is the prop that carries YOUR OWN card schemas into the browser validator; the 7 built-ins are already known. Both are objects, so both are JS properties (`el.cardSchemas = …`), never HTML attributes.\n\nWrite both once with `createCardRegistry({ use, custom })` from `@kitn.ai/ui/schemas` and thread it to both ends: `el.cardTypes = cards.tags` / `el.cardSchemas = cards.validationSchemas` on the client, `cardTools(cards, { provider })` on the route.\n\nBuilt-in card types (ask `component_reference` for one of these tags to get its schema and tool definition):\n- `artifact` → `<kai-artifact>`, tool `kai_artifact`\n- `choice` → `<kai-choice>`, tool `kai_choice`\n- `confirm` → `<kai-confirm>`, tool `kai_confirm`\n- `embed` → `<kai-embed>`, tool `kai_embed`\n- `form` → `<kai-form>`, tool `kai_form`\n- `link` → `<kai-link-preview>`, tool `kai_link`\n- `tasks` → `<kai-tasks>`, tool `kai_tasks`\n\n#### Wiring the loop\n- **Tool names are `kai_<card type>`.** `kai_artifact` produces `{ type: \"artifact\" }`. Use `isCardTool(name)` to split card calls from your own tools; never match the prefix by hand.\n- **`tool_call_id` becomes `CardEnvelope.id`, unchanged.** That is what makes a revision UPSERT: a model that re-sends the same tool call id replaces the card in place instead of rendering a second copy of it. Generating your own id breaks that silently.\n- **`cardFromToolCall(name, input, { id: call.id })`** turns the call into a renderable envelope, or returns `null` when it is one of your own tools so the loop falls through to it. It never throws.\n- **Everything above is one import:** `import { cardTools, cardFromToolCall, isCardTool } from '@kitn.ai/ui/schemas'`. The raw documents also ship as JSON (`@kitn.ai/ui/schemas/artifact.schema.json`) for a Python or Go backend.\n\n### Props (JavaScript properties)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **messages** `undefined | { id: string; role: \"user\" | \"assistant\"; parts: ({ type: \"text\"; text: string; raw?: undefined | { source: string; payload: unknown } } | { type: \"reasoning\"; text: string; label?: undefined | string; index?: undefined | number; streamId?: undefined | string; signature?: undefined | string; raw?: undefined | { source: string; payload: unknown } } | { type: \"tool\"; tool: { type: string; kind?: undefined | \"command\" | \"file-change\" | \"search\" | \"fetch\" | \"mcp\" | \"image\" | \"generic\"; state: \"input-streaming\" | \"input-available\" | \"output-available\" | \"output-error\"; input?: undefined | Record<string, unknown>; rawInput?: undefined | string; output?: undefined | Record<string, unknown>; toolCallId?: undefined | string; errorText?: undefined | string; raw?: undefined | { source: string; payload: unknown } }; raw?: undefined | { source: string; payload: unknown } } | { type: \"card\"; envelope: { type: string; id: string; data: unknown; title?: undefined | string; resolution?: undefined | { kind: \"action\"; action: string; payload?: unknown; at?: undefined | string } | { kind: \"submit\"; data: unknown; at?: undefined | string } | { kind: \"dismissed\"; at?: undefined | string } | { kind: \"expired\"; reason?: undefined | string; at?: undefined | string } }; raw?: undefined | { source: string; payload: unknown } } | { type: \"source\"; source: { id?: undefined | string; url?: undefined | string; title?: undefined | string; snippet?: undefined | string; index?: undefined | number }; raw?: undefined | { source: string; payload: unknown } } | { type: \"file\"; attachment: { id: string; type: \"file\" | \"source-document\"; filename?: undefined | string; mediaType?: undefined | string; url?: undefined | string; title?: undefined | string }; raw?: undefined | { source: string; payload: unknown } })[]; actions?: undefined | (\"copy\" | \"like\" | \"dislike\" | \"regenerate\" | \"edit\" | { id: string; label: string; icon?: undefined | string; tooltip?: undefined | string })[]; avatar?: undefined | { src?: undefined | string; fallback?: undefined | string; alt?: undefined | string }; feedback?: undefined | \"like\" | \"dislike\" }[]` ⚑ set as a JS property, not an HTML attribute\n  The full message thread to render, newest last. Each entry carries its role, ordered `parts`, and optional actions/avatar/feedback. Set as a JS property (`el.messages = [...]`); a NEW array reference per streaming chunk re-renders (mutating in place does not).\n- **loading** `undefined | false | true`\n  Show a typing indicator on the pending assistant turn. Set it while awaiting the assistant's reply.\n- **proseSize** `undefined | \"xs\" | \"sm\" | \"base\" | \"lg\"`\n  Body/prose font scale for rendered markdown (`'xs' | 'sm' | 'base' | 'lg'`). Defaults to `'sm'`.\n- **codeTheme** `undefined | string`\n  Shiki theme name for syntax-highlighted code blocks (e.g. `'github-dark-dimmed'`).\n- **codeHighlight** `undefined | false | true`\n  Enable Shiki syntax highlighting in code blocks. Turn off to render plain `<pre>` blocks (lighter, no highlighter load). Default true.\n- **actionsReveal** `undefined | \"always\" | \"hover\"`\n  Whether each message's action bar is always visible (`'always'`, default) or only revealed on hover of that message row (`'hover'`).\n- **scrollButton** `undefined | false | true`\n  Show the scroll-to-bottom button inside the scroll area. Default true.\n- **class** `undefined | string`\n  Extra classes applied to the thread's inner root.\n- **cardTypes** `undefined | Record<string, string>` ⚑ set as a JS property, not an HTML attribute\n  Optional card type -> custom-element tag overrides/additions for `card` parts (merged over the built-ins). Property: `el.cardTypes`. Typed as a plain string map (not the `CardTagMap` alias) so the generated React wrapper inlines it instead of emitting an unresolved named type.\n- **cardSchemas** `undefined | Record<string, object>` ⚑ set as a JS property, not an HTML attribute\n  JSON Schemas for the card types this app renders, keyed by envelope type. The companion of `cardTypes`, which says what DRAWS a card while this says what a VALID one looks like. An OBJECT, so it is a JS property only: `el.cardSchemas = { 'pricing-table': pricingSchema }`, never an attribute. `createCardRegistry(...).validationSchemas` is exactly this shape. Without it the kit validates its own seven built-ins and leaves your own card type, the one your app actually cares about, as the only unchecked thing on screen. A schema here WINS over a built-in of the same name. Typed `Record<string, object>` rather than `Record<string, JsonSchema>` deliberately: an imported `.json` schema widens `\"type\"` to `string`, and an authored one carries `$schema`/`title`/`description`/`additionalProperties`, so the tighter type would reject both of the normal ways to supply one.\n\n### Attributes (HTML-safe)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **loading** `undefined | false | true`\n  Show a typing indicator on the pending assistant turn. Set it while awaiting the assistant's reply.\n- **prose-size** `undefined | \"xs\" | \"sm\" | \"base\" | \"lg\"`\n  Body/prose font scale for rendered markdown (`'xs' | 'sm' | 'base' | 'lg'`). Defaults to `'sm'`.\n- **code-theme** `undefined | string`\n  Shiki theme name for syntax-highlighted code blocks (e.g. `'github-dark-dimmed'`).\n- **code-highlight** `undefined | false | true`\n  Enable Shiki syntax highlighting in code blocks. Turn off to render plain `<pre>` blocks (lighter, no highlighter load). Default true.\n- **actions-reveal** `undefined | \"always\" | \"hover\"`\n  Whether each message's action bar is always visible (`'always'`, default) or only revealed on hover of that message row (`'hover'`).\n- **scroll-button** `undefined | false | true`\n  Show the scroll-to-bottom button inside the scroll area. Default true.\n- **class** `undefined | string`\n  Extra classes applied to the thread's inner root.\n\n### Events (CustomEvent, listen via addEventListener)\n- **kai-message-action** — A message's action button was clicked. `action` is the built-in name (`copy` / `like` / `dislike` / `regenerate` / `edit`) or a custom id. `state` is present only for the toggleable feedback votes: `'on'` when a like/dislike is set, `'off'` when re-tapped to clear. `detail`: `{ messageId: string; action: string; state?: undefined | \"on\" | \"off\" }`\n\n### Methods (call these on the element instance)\nGet the element (`const el = document.querySelector('kai-thread')`), then call it. Methods drive the element from your code; the events above report back what the user did. Both are public API.\n- **scrollToBottom(behavior?: ScrollBehavior): void** — Scroll the message list to the bottom (default `'smooth'`).\n\n### Composition slots\nProject your own markup into these named regions — add a light-DOM child with a `slot=\"…\"` attribute (e.g. `<div slot=\"sidebar\">…</div>`).\n- **empty** — Custom zero-state rendered in the message area while the thread is empty; replaces the built-in default.\n\n### Invariants\nRules that have already broken real consumers of this kit. Each block says what enforces it — read that line rather than assuming CI catches a violation, because nothing here reads YOUR code. 3 of the 6 below are enforced by NOTHING at all, and 2 more by only half of what they say. Diagnosis and wrong/right examples for every invariant are served once, not repeated per element — call component_reference with { name: \"invariants\" }.\n\n#### props-not-attributes — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nSet arrays, objects and functions as JS PROPERTIES on the element instance. Only scalars (strings, numbers, booleans) belong in attributes, and the derived layer's scalar flag records which prop is which. What actually goes wrong, because the mechanism is not the obvious one: a framework template binding or String() stringifies an array to '[object Object]', which is not JSON, and the attribute path falls back to handing the element that raw STRING — so the prop is silently a string and the list renders nothing. A function cannot survive JSON at all: JSON.stringify({ onSubmit }) is '{}', so every callback is dropped. And an attribute re-set is not how updates are delivered — see reactivity-two-halves. A hand-written, valid-JSON attribute does happen to parse today, because a transitive dependency JSON.parses attributes whose declared default is not a string, but that is that dependency's behaviour and not this kit's contract; do not build on it. NOTHING IN THIS REPO ENFORCES THIS — it is a consumer contract, and the scalar flag in the derived layer is how the catalog SERVES the fact, which is not the same as checking it.\n\n#### events-non-bubbling — enforced by a structural guarantee in packages/ui/src/elements/define.tsx\nNon-bubbling is the default: public kai-* events are dispatched through the one helper that hard-codes bubbles:false and composed:false, so listen on the element itself, never on a parent or document. The protocol exceptions (kai-maximize-intent, kai-maximize-state and kai-card) bubble or compose deliberately and are listed in the derived layer under eventExceptions — do not generalise from them to the rest.\n\n#### host-coordinates — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nThere is no store. Data flows in via properties, out via events, and the host wires element A to element B. Solid context does not cross element boundaries, so nothing coordinates elements except the host application, and no element owns another. Placing two elements in the same subtree wires nothing.\n\n#### untrusted-model-output — PARTIALLY ENFORCED: one half is covered, by the kit's own tests (packages/ui/tests/components/markdown-xss.test.tsx, packages/ui/tests/components/artifact-url-xss.test.tsx, packages/ui/tests/components/hostile-model-output.test.tsx); the statement says which half is not\nEverything the model produced is untrusted input: a MessagePart, card envelope or tool argument reaching innerHTML, an href or src, window.open or an iframe is a vulnerability. THE DEFECT IS NEVER A MISSING GUARD, IT IS WHICH PATH GOT IT — every one found so far sat on a path the CONSUMER controls while the model-controlled path beside it had none. So put a policy on the sink, and MATCH THE LIST TO THE SINK rather than reaching for one universal list: http:, https: and mailto: for anything navigable the user may click, resolved against the page so ordinary relative links still work; http: and https: ONLY for a model-supplied citation, which is a reference to a page on the public web and has no business being relative. Those are two lists because there are two sinks — it is the same split the kit makes internally between SAFE_SCHEMES and RENDERABLE_SCHEMES, not a variant invented here. Whichever you use, parse inside a try/catch and RETURN FALSE on an unparseable URL: new URL() throws, and a throw at a sink crashes the render. And render model text as TEXT. Escaping is the correct rendering: the source text must stay VISIBLE as well as inert, because a filter that deleted it would pass the security check and be a worse UI. COVERAGE, and read this before trusting CI here: the three XSS suites are tests and ONLY tests. They run in the required test job, so the vectors they pin cannot come back — but NOTHING structural stops a NEW sink landing unguarded. No lint script in the package is about sinks, and the coupling map's unenforced list has no entry for the class. A new sink is caught in review or not at all.\n\n#### kit-parses-consumer-fetches — PARTIALLY ENFORCED: one half is covered, by the kit's `lint:silent-drops` script, in required CI; the statement says which half is not\nThe kit parses; the consumer fetches. Two halves, covered differently. KIT SIDE: every MessagePart variant the wire encodes must be accounted for, or a variant is gone once the request leaves — that half is enforced by lint:silent-drops in CI. CONSUMER SIDE: never hand-roll an SSE reader; import readOpenAIStream, readAnthropicStream or readModelStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire, and fetch from your own endpoint, because there is no client, no key handling and no provider SDK below wire/. NO CI CHECK COVERS THAT SECOND HALF — no guard reads consumer or scaffolded code for a hand-rolled reader; it is measured by the acceptance deck instead, at scenario S2, whose scoring line is 'imports readOpenAIStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire; no hand-rolled SSE reader anywhere in the output'.\n\n#### upgrade-race (script-tag delivery only) — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this, until issue #99 option B lands in defineWebComponent. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nA property set before the element upgrades is lost. On script-tag targets, load order is not ours. Until issue #99 option B (upgrade-property preservation in defineWebComponent) lands, every script-tag recipe must state this race loudly and set properties only after registration — await customElements.whenDefined(tag), which is the guarantee. A timer, or DOMContentLoaded, is a guess about load order rather than a guarantee: registration can land later, from an async chunk or a dynamically inserted script."}]}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ca9e799f-3e0b-44f3-8c23-c900a54d324e","uuid":"389d4a7f-01ef-40bb-b588-0d6d672e280e","timestamp":"2026-08-25T12:16:51.278Z","tool_use_result":[{"type":"text","text":"## <kai-thread>\n\n### Getting the element\nRegister it before you use it. If you skip registering it the element never upgrades: nothing renders, **no error and no warning is logged**, a property you set assigns and reads back correctly, and `customElements.whenDefined('kai-thread')` never resolves.\n\n```ts\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements/thread'; // or just this one\n```\n\nTypeScript: `import type { KaiThreadElement } from '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';` — the element interface ships with the package; do not hand-roll a structural type.\n\n### AI/UI contract\n`kai-*` elements accept **array and object data as JavaScript properties** (set in JavaScript via `el.property = value`, not as HTML attributes). Events are native CustomEvents — listen with `el.addEventListener(\"event-name\", handler)` and read `event.detail` for the payload.\n\n### Card contract\n`<kai-thread>` renders generative-UI cards inside the thread. It is a HOST: it draws whatever envelopes arrive, and carries the two props that say what a card may be.\n\n- **`cardTypes`** — `Record<envelope type, custom element tag>`. What DRAWS a card. Merged over the built-ins, so your entry overrides ours and the other 6 still render.\n- **`cardSchemas`** — `Record<envelope type, JSON Schema>`. What a VALID card looks like. This is the prop that carries YOUR OWN card schemas into the browser validator; the 7 built-ins are already known. Both are objects, so both are JS properties (`el.cardSchemas = …`), never HTML attributes.\n\nWrite both once with `createCardRegistry({ use, custom })` from `@kitn.ai/ui/schemas` and thread it to both ends: `el.cardTypes = cards.tags` / `el.cardSchemas = cards.validationSchemas` on the client, `cardTools(cards, { provider })` on the route.\n\nBuilt-in card types (ask `component_reference` for one of these tags to get its schema and tool definition):\n- `artifact` → `<kai-artifact>`, tool `kai_artifact`\n- `choice` → `<kai-choice>`, tool `kai_choice`\n- `confirm` → `<kai-confirm>`, tool `kai_confirm`\n- `embed` → `<kai-embed>`, tool `kai_embed`\n- `form` → `<kai-form>`, tool `kai_form`\n- `link` → `<kai-link-preview>`, tool `kai_link`\n- `tasks` → `<kai-tasks>`, tool `kai_tasks`\n\n#### Wiring the loop\n- **Tool names are `kai_<card type>`.** `kai_artifact` produces `{ type: \"artifact\" }`. Use `isCardTool(name)` to split card calls from your own tools; never match the prefix by hand.\n- **`tool_call_id` becomes `CardEnvelope.id`, unchanged.** That is what makes a revision UPSERT: a model that re-sends the same tool call id replaces the card in place instead of rendering a second copy of it. Generating your own id breaks that silently.\n- **`cardFromToolCall(name, input, { id: call.id })`** turns the call into a renderable envelope, or returns `null` when it is one of your own tools so the loop falls through to it. It never throws.\n- **Everything above is one import:** `import { cardTools, cardFromToolCall, isCardTool } from '@kitn.ai/ui/schemas'`. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01E9jzL1vos6fTDuh53NAbj3","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"## <kai-composer>\n\n### Getting the element\nRegister it before you use it. If you skip registering it the element never upgrades: nothing renders, **no error and no warning is logged**, a property you set assigns and reads back correctly, and `customElements.whenDefined('kai-composer')` never resolves.\n\n```ts\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements/composer'; // or just this one\n```\n\nTypeScript: `import type { KaiComposerElement } from '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';` — the element interface ships with the package; do not hand-roll a structural type.\n\n### AI/UI contract\n`kai-*` elements accept **array and object data as JavaScript properties** (set in JavaScript via `el.property = value`, not as HTML attributes). Events are native CustomEvents — listen with `el.addEventListener(\"event-name\", handler)` and read `event.detail` for the payload.\n\n### Props (JavaScript properties)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **value** `undefined | string | ({ type: \"text\"; text: string } | { type: \"entity\"; entity: { kind: string; id: string; label: string; icon?: undefined | string; promptText?: undefined | string; data?: undefined | Record<string, unknown> } })[]` ⚑ set as a JS property, not an HTML attribute\n  Controlled value: a string or a full ComposerDoc (set as JS property).\n- **placeholder** `undefined | string`\n  Placeholder text shown when the composer is empty.\n- **disabled** `undefined | false | true`\n  Disable the composer entirely (non-interactive).\n- **loading** `undefined | false | true`\n  Show a loading/streaming state and block submit.\n- **maxHeight** `undefined | number | string`\n  Maximum height in px before the content scrolls. Default 240.\n- **submitOnEnter** `undefined | false | true`\n  Whether pressing Enter (without Shift) submits. Default true.\n- **triggers** `undefined | { char: string; kind: string; items?: undefined | { id: string; label: string; icon?: undefined | string; description?: undefined | string; group?: undefined | string; kind?: undefined | string; promptText?: undefined | string; data?: undefined | Record<string, unknown> }[] }[]` ⚑ set as a JS property, not an HTML attribute\n  Trigger definitions. Set as a JS property.\n- **highlights** `undefined | (string | { pattern: string; flags?: undefined | string; class?: undefined | string })[]` ⚑ set as a JS property, not an HTML attribute\n  Keyword highlight rules. Set as a JS property.\n- **kindIcons** `undefined | Record<string, string>` ⚑ set as a JS property, not an HTML attribute\n  Default icon per entity kind (kind → image URL/data-URI) for items without their own `icon`. Overrides the built-in agent/plugin glyphs. JS property.\n\n### Attributes (HTML-safe)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **placeholder** `undefined | string`\n  Placeholder text shown when the composer is empty.\n- **disabled** `undefined | false | true`\n  Disable the composer entirely (non-interactive).\n- **loading** `undefined | false | true`\n  Show a loading/streaming state and block submit.\n- **max-height** `undefined | number | string`\n  Maximum height in px before the content scrolls. Default 240.\n- **submit-on-enter** `undefined | false | true`\n  Whether pressing Enter (without Shift) submits. Default true.\n\n### Events (CustomEvent, listen via addEventListener)\n- **kai-blur** — The composer lost focus. `detail`: `{ originalEvent: FocusEvent }`\n- **kai-entity-add** — An entity pill was inserted into the composer. `detail`: `{ entity: { kind: string; id: string; label: string; icon?: undefined | string; promptText?: undefined | string; data?: undefined | Record<string, unknown> } }`\n- **kai-entity-remove** — An entity pill was deleted from the composer. `detail`: `{ entity: { kind: string; id: string; label: string; icon?: undefined | string; promptText?: undefined | string; data?: undefined | Record<string, unknown> } }`\n- **kai-focus** — The composer gained focus. `focus`/`blur` are NOT composed natively, so they don't escape the shadow root; these re-expose them on the host. (For `keydown`/`paste`/`focusin`/`focusout`, listen NATIVELY on `<kai-composer>`: they're composed and already cross the shadow boundary.) `detail`: `{ originalEvent: FocusEvent }`\n- **kai-submit** — The user submitted the composer (Enter or programmatic submit). `detail`: `{ doc: ({ type: \"text\"; text: string } | { type: \"entity\"; entity: { kind: string; id: string; label: string; icon?: undefined | string; promptText?: undefined | string; data?: undefined | Record<string, unknown> } })[]; text: string; entities: { kind: string; id: string; label: string; icon?: undefined | string; promptText?: undefined | string; data?: undefined | Record<string, unknown> }[] }`\n- **kai-trigger** — A trigger character was detected at the caret (e.g. `/` or `@`). `detail`: `{ char: string; query: string; rect: DOMRect }`\n- **kai-trigger-close** — The active trigger was dismissed (Escape, space, or outside click). `detail`: `Record<string, never>`\n- **kai-value-change** — The content changed (fires on every input event). `detail`: `{ doc: ({ type: \"text\"; text: string } | { type: \"entity\"; entity: { kind: string; id: string; label: string; icon?: undefined | string; promptText?: undefined | string; data?: undefined | Record<string, unknown> } })[]; text: string; entities: { kind: string; id: string; label: string; icon?: undefined | string; promptText?: undefined | string; data?: undefined | Record<string, unknown> }[] }`\n\n### Methods (call these on the element instance)\nGet the element (`const el = document.querySelector('kai-composer')`), then call it. Methods drive the element from your code; the events above report back what the user did. Both are public API.\n- **focus(options?: FocusOptions): void** — Focus the editable element. `focus`/`blur` are NOT composed natively, so a host-level focus() can't reach the editable inside the shadow root. This is the only way to focus the composer programmatically.\n- **blur(): void** — Blur the editable element.\n- **clear(): void** — Empty the composer to a blank doc (resets the internal value + history baseline; fires kai-value-change).\n- **send(): void** — Submit the current content programmatically, on the same path as Enter (fires kai-submit). Named `send`, not `submit`, to match the shared vocabulary and avoid any submit collision.\n- **insertEntity(entity: EntityRef): void** — Insert an atomic entity pill (skill/agent/plugin) at the caret without typing a trigger (fires kai-entity-add).\n\n### Invariants\nRules that have already broken real consumers of this kit. Each block says what enforces it — read that line rather than assuming CI catches a violation, because nothing here reads YOUR code. 3 of the 6 below are enforced by NOTHING at all, and 2 more by only half of what they say. Diagnosis and wrong/right examples for every invariant are served once, not repeated per element — call component_reference with { name: \"invariants\" }.\n\n#### props-not-attributes — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nSet arrays, objects and functions as JS PROPERTIES on the element instance. Only scalars (strings, numbers, booleans) belong in attributes, and the derived layer's scalar flag records which prop is which. What actually goes wrong, because the mechanism is not the obvious one: a framework template binding or String() stringifies an array to '[object Object]', which is not JSON, and the attribute path falls back to handing the element that raw STRING — so the prop is silently a string and the list renders nothing. A function cannot survive JSON at all: JSON.stringify({ onSubmit }) is '{}', so every callback is dropped. And an attribute re-set is not how updates are delivered — see reactivity-two-halves. A hand-written, valid-JSON attribute does happen to parse today, because a transitive dependency JSON.parses attributes whose declared default is not a string, but that is that dependency's behaviour and not this kit's contract; do not build on it. NOTHING IN THIS REPO ENFORCES THIS — it is a consumer contract, and the scalar flag in the derived layer is how the catalog SERVES the fact, which is not the same as checking it.\n\n#### events-non-bubbling — enforced by a structural guarantee in packages/ui/src/elements/define.tsx\nNon-bubbling is the default: public kai-* events are dispatched through the one helper that hard-codes bubbles:false and composed:false, so listen on the element itself, never on a parent or document. The protocol exceptions (kai-maximize-intent, kai-maximize-state and kai-card) bubble or compose deliberately and are listed in the derived layer under eventExceptions — do not generalise from them to the rest.\n\n#### host-coordinates — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nThere is no store. Data flows in via properties, out via events, and the host wires element A to element B. Solid context does not cross element boundaries, so nothing coordinates elements except the host application, and no element owns another. Placing two elements in the same subtree wires nothing.\n\n#### untrusted-model-output — PARTIALLY ENFORCED: one half is covered, by the kit's own tests (packages/ui/tests/components/markdown-xss.test.tsx, packages/ui/tests/components/artifact-url-xss.test.tsx, packages/ui/tests/components/hostile-model-output.test.tsx); the statement says which half is not\nEverything the model produced is untrusted input: a MessagePart, card envelope or tool argument reaching innerHTML, an href or src, window.open or an iframe is a vulnerability. THE DEFECT IS NEVER A MISSING GUARD, IT IS WHICH PATH GOT IT — every one found so far sat on a path the CONSUMER controls while the model-controlled path beside it had none. So put a policy on the sink, and MATCH THE LIST TO THE SINK rather than reaching for one universal list: http:, https: and mailto: for anything navigable the user may click, resolved against the page so ordinary relative links still work; http: and https: ONLY for a model-supplied citation, which is a reference to a page on the public web and has no business being relative. Those are two lists because there are two sinks — it is the same split the kit makes internally between SAFE_SCHEMES and RENDERABLE_SCHEMES, not a variant invented here. Whichever you use, parse inside a try/catch and RETURN FALSE on an unparseable URL: new URL() throws, and a throw at a sink crashes the render. And render model text as TEXT. Escaping is the correct rendering: the source text must stay VISIBLE as well as inert, because a filter that deleted it would pass the security check and be a worse UI. COVERAGE, and read this before trusting CI here: the three XSS suites are tests and ONLY tests. They run in the required test job, so the vectors they pin cannot come back — but NOTHING structural stops a NEW sink landing unguarded. No lint script in the package is about sinks, and the coupling map's unenforced list has no entry for the class. A new sink is caught in review or not at all.\n\n#### kit-parses-consumer-fetches — PARTIALLY ENFORCED: one half is covered, by the kit's `lint:silent-drops` script, in required CI; the statement says which half is not\nThe kit parses; the consumer fetches. Two halves, covered differently. KIT SIDE: every MessagePart variant the wire encodes must be accounted for, or a variant is gone once the request leaves — that half is enforced by lint:silent-drops in CI. CONSUMER SIDE: never hand-roll an SSE reader; import readOpenAIStream, readAnthropicStream or readModelStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire, and fetch from your own endpoint, because there is no client, no key handling and no provider SDK below wire/. NO CI CHECK COVERS THAT SECOND HALF — no guard reads consumer or scaffolded code for a hand-rolled reader; it is measured by the acceptance deck instead, at scenario S2, whose scoring line is 'imports readOpenAIStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire; no hand-rolled SSE reader anywhere in the output'.\n\n#### upgrade-race (script-tag delivery only) — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this, until issue #99 option B lands in defineWebComponent. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nA property set before the element upgrades is lost. On script-tag targets, load order is not ours. Until issue #99 option B (upgrade-property preservation in defineWebComponent) lands, every script-tag recipe must state this race loudly and set properties only after registration — await customElements.whenDefined(tag), which is the guarantee. A timer, or DOMContentLoaded, is a guess about load order rather than a guarantee: registration can land later, from an async chunk or a dynamically inserted script."}]}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ca9e799f-3e0b-44f3-8c23-c900a54d324e","uuid":"0f5d4617-778d-4703-81df-da47fea70e5a","timestamp":"2026-08-25T12:16:54.679Z","tool_use_result":[{"type":"text","text":"## <kai-composer>\n\n### Getting the element\nRegister it before you use it.
