Whichever you use, parse inside a try/catch and RETURN FALSE on an unparseable URL: new URL() throws, and a throw at a sink crashes the render. And render model text as TEXT. Escaping is the correct rendering: the source text must stay VISIBLE as well as inert, because a filter that deleted it would pass the security check and be a worse UI. COVERAGE, and read this before trusting CI here: the three XSS suites are tests and ONLY tests. They run in the required test job, so the vectors they pin cannot come back — but NOTHING structural stops a NEW sink landing unguarded. No lint script in the package is about sinks, and the coupling map's unenforced list has no entry for the class. A new sink is caught in review or not at all.\n\n#### kit-parses-consumer-fetches — PARTIALLY ENFORCED: one half is covered, by the kit's `lint:silent-drops` script, in required CI; the statement says which half is not\nThe kit parses; the consumer fetches. Two halves, covered differently. KIT SIDE: every MessagePart variant the wire encodes must be accounted for, or a variant is gone once the request leaves — that half is enforced by lint:silent-drops in CI. CONSUMER SIDE: never hand-roll an SSE reader; import readOpenAIStream, readAnthropicStream or readModelStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire, and fetch from your own endpoint, because there is no client, no key handling and no provider SDK below wire/. NO CI CHECK COVERS THAT SECOND HALF — no guard reads consumer or scaffolded code for a hand-rolled reader; it is measured by the acceptance deck instead, at scenario S2, whose scoring line is 'imports readOpenAIStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire; no hand-rolled SSE reader anywhere in the output'.\n\n#### upgrade-race (script-tag delivery only) — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this, until issue #99 option B lands in defineWebComponent. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nA property set before the element upgrades is lost. On script-tag targets, load order is not ours. Until issue #99 option B (upgrade-property preservation in defineWebComponent) lands, every script-tag recipe must state this race loudly and set properties only after registration — await customElements.whenDefined(tag), which is the guarantee. A timer, or DOMContentLoaded, is a guess about load order rather than a guarantee: registration can land later, from an async chunk or a dynamically inserted script."}]}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01UfziwCXH2A6j7cM7qD7Wcx","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"## <kai-attachments>\n\n### Getting the element\nRegister it before you use it. If you skip registering it the element never upgrades: nothing renders, **no error and no warning is logged**, a property you set assigns and reads back correctly, and `customElements.whenDefined('kai-attachments')` never resolves.\n\n```ts\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements/attachments'; // or just this one\n```\n\nTypeScript: `import type { KaiAttachmentsElement } from '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';` — the element interface ships with the package; do not hand-roll a structural type.\n\n### AI/UI contract\n`kai-*` elements accept **array and object data as JavaScript properties** (set in JavaScript via `el.property = value`, not as HTML attributes). Events are native CustomEvents — listen with `el.addEventListener(\"event-name\", handler)` and read `event.detail` for the payload.\n\n### Props (JavaScript properties)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **items** `undefined | { id: string; type: \"file\" | \"source-document\"; filename?: undefined | string; mediaType?: undefined | string; url?: undefined | string; title?: undefined | string }[]` ⚑ set as a JS property, not an HTML attribute\n  The attachments to render. Omit (or pass an empty array) for the empty state, which shows `emptyText` if set and nothing otherwise. Set as a JS property (array).\n- **variant** `undefined | \"grid\" | \"inline\" | \"list\"`\n  Layout: `grid` = visual tiles, `inline` = icon + label chips, `list` = rows.\n- **hoverCard** `undefined | false | true`\n  Wrap each item in a hover card that previews its details.\n- **removable** `undefined | false | true`\n  Show a remove button per item; clicking it fires a `kai-remove` event.\n- **showMediaType** `undefined | false | true`\n  Also show the media type beneath the filename (non-grid variants).\n- **emptyText** `undefined | string`\n  Text shown when `items` is empty.\n\n### Attributes (HTML-safe)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **variant** `undefined | \"grid\" | \"inline\" | \"list\"`\n  Layout: `grid` = visual tiles, `inline` = icon + label chips, `list` = rows.\n- **hover-card** `undefined | false | true`\n  Wrap each item in a hover card that previews its details.\n- **removable** `undefined | false | true`\n  Show a remove button per item; clicking it fires a `kai-remove` event.\n- **show-media-type** `undefined | false | true`\n  Also show the media type beneath the filename (non-grid variants).\n- **empty-text** `undefined | string`\n  Text shown when `items` is empty.\n\n### Events (CustomEvent, listen via addEventListener)\n- **kai-remove** — A remove button was clicked. `detail`: `{ id: string }`\n\n### Styleable parts (`::part`)\nRestyle these from outside the Shadow DOM via `kai-attachments::part(name) { … }`.\n- **preview** — The image shown in an attachment’s hover-card preview. Bounded by default (max ~320×256, aspect preserved) so a large image never blows up the card. Raise or lower the cap from outside.\n  ```css\n  kai-attachments::part(preview) { max-width: 32rem; max-height: 24rem }\n  ```\n- **attachment** — One attachment item: the chip, row or tile, whichever variant is rendering. Restyle its background, radius or border from outside without caring which layout it is.\n  ```css\n  kai-chat::part(attachment) { border-radius: 0.25rem }\n  ```\n- **attachment-name** — The attachment’s filename label. Present in every variant that shows one (a grid tile omits it for an image, which is its own label). Retune its type or hide it entirely.\n  ```css\n  kai-chat::part(attachment-name) { font-size: 0.75rem }\n  ```\n\n### Invariants\nRules that have already broken real consumers of this kit. Each block says what enforces it — read that line rather than assuming CI catches a violation, because nothing here reads YOUR code. 3 of the 6 below are enforced by NOTHING at all, and 2 more by only half of what they say. Diagnosis and wrong/right examples for every invariant are served once, not repeated per element — call component_reference with { name: \"invariants\" }.\n\n#### props-not-attributes — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nSet arrays, objects and functions as JS PROPERTIES on the element instance. Only scalars (strings, numbers, booleans) belong in attributes, and the derived layer's scalar flag records which prop is which. What actually goes wrong, because the mechanism is not the obvious one: a framework template binding or String() stringifies an array to '[object Object]', which is not JSON, and the attribute path falls back to handing the element that raw STRING — so the prop is silently a string and the list renders nothing. A function cannot survive JSON at all: JSON.stringify({ onSubmit }) is '{}', so every callback is dropped. And an attribute re-set is not how updates are delivered — see reactivity-two-halves. A hand-written, valid-JSON attribute does happen to parse today, because a transitive dependency JSON.parses attributes whose declared default is not a string, but that is that dependency's behaviour and not this kit's contract; do not build on it. NOTHING IN THIS REPO ENFORCES THIS — it is a consumer contract, and the scalar flag in the derived layer is how the catalog SERVES the fact, which is not the same as checking it.\n\n#### events-non-bubbling — enforced by a structural guarantee in packages/ui/src/elements/define.tsx\nNon-bubbling is the default: public kai-* events are dispatched through the one helper that hard-codes bubbles:false and composed:false, so listen on the element itself, never on a parent or document. The protocol exceptions (kai-maximize-intent, kai-maximize-state and kai-card) bubble or compose deliberately and are listed in the derived layer under eventExceptions — do not generalise from them to the rest.\n\n#### host-coordinates — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nThere is no store. Data flows in via properties, out via events, and the host wires element A to element B. Solid context does not cross element boundaries, so nothing coordinates elements except the host application, and no element owns another. Placing two elements in the same subtree wires nothing.\n\n#### untrusted-model-output — PARTIALLY ENFORCED: one half is covered, by the kit's own tests (packages/ui/tests/components/markdown-xss.test.tsx, packages/ui/tests/components/artifact-url-xss.test.tsx, packages/ui/tests/components/hostile-model-output.test.tsx); the statement says which half is not\nEverything the model produced is untrusted input: a MessagePart, card envelope or tool argument reaching innerHTML, an href or src, window.open or an iframe is a vulnerability. THE DEFECT IS NEVER A MISSING GUARD, IT IS WHICH PATH GOT IT — every one found so far sat on a path the CONSUMER controls while the model-controlled path beside it had none. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HYDHPa1Z2kLo6DX7N7cZpU","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"## <kai-message>\n\n### Getting the element\nRegister it before you use it. If you skip registering it the element never upgrades: nothing renders, **no error and no warning is logged**, a property you set assigns and reads back correctly, and `customElements.whenDefined('kai-message')` never resolves.\n\n```ts\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements/message'; // or just this one\n```\n\nTypeScript: `import type { KaiMessageElement } from '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';` — the element interface ships with the package; do not hand-roll a structural type.\n\n### AI/UI contract\n`kai-*` elements accept **array and object data as JavaScript properties** (set in JavaScript via `el.property = value`, not as HTML attributes). Events are native CustomEvents — listen with `el.addEventListener(\"event-name\", handler)` and read `event.detail` for the payload.\n\n### Card contract\n`<kai-message>` renders generative-UI cards inside the thread. It is a HOST: it draws whatever envelopes arrive, and carries the two props that say what a card may be.\n\n- **`cardTypes`** — `Record<envelope type, custom element tag>`. What DRAWS a card. Merged over the built-ins, so your entry overrides ours and the other 6 still render.\n- **`cardSchemas`** — `Record<envelope type, JSON Schema>`. What a VALID card looks like. This is the prop that carries YOUR OWN card schemas into the browser validator; the 7 built-ins are already known. Both are objects, so both are JS properties (`el.cardSchemas = …`), never HTML attributes.\n\nWrite both once with `createCardRegistry({ use, custom })` from `@kitn.ai/ui/schemas` and thread it to both ends: `el.cardTypes = cards.tags` / `el.cardSchemas = cards.validationSchemas` on the client, `cardTools(cards, { provider })` on the route.\n\nBuilt-in card types (ask `component_reference` for one of these tags to get its schema and tool definition):\n- `artifact` → `<kai-artifact>`, tool `kai_artifact`\n- `choice` → `<kai-choice>`, tool `kai_choice`\n- `confirm` → `<kai-confirm>`, tool `kai_confirm`\n- `embed` → `<kai-embed>`, tool `kai_embed`\n- `form` → `<kai-form>`, tool `kai_form`\n- `link` → `<kai-link-preview>`, tool `kai_link`\n- `tasks` → `<kai-tasks>`, tool `kai_tasks`\n\n#### Wiring the loop\n- **Tool names are `kai_<card type>`.** `kai_artifact` produces `{ type: \"artifact\" }`. Use `isCardTool(name)` to split card calls from your own tools; never match the prefix by hand.\n- **`tool_call_id` becomes `CardEnvelope.id`, unchanged.** That is what makes a revision UPSERT: a model that re-sends the same tool call id replaces the card in place instead of rendering a second copy of it. Generating your own id breaks that silently.\n- **`cardFromToolCall(name, input, { id: call.id })`** turns the call into a renderable envelope, or returns `null` when it is one of your own tools so the loop falls through to it. It never throws.\n- **Everything above is one import:** `import { cardTools, cardFromToolCall, isCardTool } from '@kitn.ai/ui/schemas'`. The raw documents also ship as JSON (`@kitn.ai/ui/schemas/artifact.schema.json`) for a Python or Go backend.\n\n### Props (JavaScript properties)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **message** `undefined | { id: string; role: \"user\" | \"assistant\"; parts: ({ type: \"text\"; text: string; raw?: undefined | { source: string; payload: unknown } } | { type: \"reasoning\"; text: string; label?: undefined | string; index?: undefined | number; streamId?: undefined | string; signature?: undefined | string; raw?: undefined | { source: string; payload: unknown } } | { type: \"tool\"; tool: { type: string; kind?: undefined | \"command\" | \"file-change\" | \"search\" | \"fetch\" | \"mcp\" | \"image\" | \"generic\"; state: \"input-streaming\" | \"input-available\" | \"output-available\" | \"output-error\"; input?: undefined | Record<string, unknown>; rawInput?: undefined | string; output?: undefined | Record<string, unknown>; toolCallId?: undefined | string; errorText?: undefined | string; raw?: undefined | { source: string; payload: unknown } }; raw?: undefined | { source: string; payload: unknown } } | { type: \"card\"; envelope: { type: string; id: string; data: unknown; title?: undefined | string; resolution?: undefined | { kind: \"action\"; action: string; payload?: unknown; at?: undefined | string } | { kind: \"submit\"; data: unknown; at?: undefined | string } | { kind: \"dismissed\"; at?: undefined | string } | { kind: \"expired\"; reason?: undefined | string; at?: undefined | string } }; raw?: undefined | { source: string; payload: unknown } } | { type: \"source\"; source: { id?: undefined | string; url?: undefined | string; title?: undefined | string; snippet?: undefined | string; index?: undefined | number }; raw?: undefined | { source: string; payload: unknown } } | { type: \"file\"; attachment: { id: string; type: \"file\" | \"source-document\"; filename?: undefined | string; mediaType?: undefined | string; url?: undefined | string; title?: undefined | string }; raw?: undefined | { source: string; payload: unknown } })[]; actions?: undefined | (\"copy\" | \"like\" | \"dislike\" | \"regenerate\" | \"edit\" | { id: string; label: string; icon?: undefined | string; tooltip?: undefined | string })[]; avatar?: undefined | { src?: undefined | string; fallback?: undefined | string; alt?: undefined | string }; feedback?: undefined | \"like\" | \"dislike\" }` ⚑ set as a JS property, not an HTML attribute\n  The full message object. Set as a JS property.\n- **role** `undefined | \"user\" | \"assistant\"`\n  Who is speaking: `'user'` or `'assistant'`. Convenience for simple cases when not passing a `message` object. This is the SEMANTIC role of the message, not an ARIA role. The name collides with the global ARIA `role` attribute, which is why the facade lifts it off the host (see `liftRoleOffHost`). Neither speaker is a valid ARIA role, so a `role=\"user\"` left on `<kai-message>` is a CRITICAL axe `aria-roles` violation. The accessible role lives on the row inside the shadow root instead: `role=\"article\"` plus an `aria-label` naming the speaker, matching the SolidJS `<Message>` component.\n- **markdown** `undefined | false | true`\n  Force markdown on/off. Defaults to on for assistant, off for user.\n- **proseSize** `undefined | \"xs\" | \"sm\" | \"base\" | \"lg\"`\n  Text/markdown sizing for the message body.\n- **codeTheme** `undefined | string`\n  Shiki theme name used for fenced code blocks in the content.\n- **codeHighlight** `undefined | false | true`\n  Disable syntax highlighting for code blocks (no Shiki loads).\n- **actionsReveal** `undefined | \"always\" | \"hover\"`\n  Whether the action bar is always visible (`'always'`, default) or only revealed on hover of the message row (`'hover'`).\n- **avatarSrc** `undefined | string`\n  Convenience avatar image URL (used when `message.avatar` is not set).\n- **avatarFallback** `undefined | string`\n  Convenience avatar fallback text (used when `message.avatar` is not set).\n- **avatar** `undefined | string`\n  Avatar rail mode. `'none'` omits the avatar rail entirely so the body spans the full row (predictable layout when you never show avatars). Any other value keeps the default behaviour: the built-in avatar when one resolves, or your `slot=\"avatar\"` content when projected (which REPLACES the built-in).\n- **cardTypes** `undefined | Record<string, string>` ⚑ set as a JS property, not an HTML attribute\n  Optional card type -> custom-element tag overrides/additions for `card` parts (merged over the built-ins). Property: `el.cardTypes`. Typed as a plain string map (not the `CardTagMap` alias) so the generated React wrapper inlines it instead of emitting an unresolved named type.\n- **cardSchemas** `undefined | Record<string, object>` ⚑ set as a JS property, not an HTML attribute\n  JSON Schemas for the card types this app renders, keyed by envelope type. The companion of `cardTypes`, which says what DRAWS a card while this says what a VALID one looks like. An OBJECT, so it is a JS property only: `el.cardSchemas = { 'pricing-table': pricingSchema }`, never an attribute. `createCardRegistry(...).validationSchemas` is exactly this shape. Without it the kit validates its own seven built-ins and leaves your own card type, the one your app actually cares about, as the only unchecked thing on screen. A schema here WINS over a built-in of the same name. Typed `Record<string, object>` rather than `Record<string, JsonSchema>` deliberately: an imported `.json` schema widens `\"type\"` to `string`, and an authored one carries `$schema`/`title`/`description`/`additionalProperties`, so the tighter type would reject both of the normal ways to supply one.\n\n### Attributes (HTML-safe)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **role** `undefined | \"user\" | \"assistant\"`\n  Who is speaking: `'user'` or `'assistant'`. Convenience for simple cases when not passing a `message` object. This is the SEMANTIC role of the message, not an ARIA role. The name collides with the global ARIA `role` attribute, which is why the facade lifts it off the host (see `liftRoleOffHost`). Neither speaker is a valid ARIA role, so a `role=\"user\"` left on `<kai-message>` is a CRITICAL axe `aria-roles` violation. The accessible role lives on the row inside the shadow root instead: `role=\"article\"` plus an `aria-label` naming the speaker, matching the SolidJS `<Message>` component.\n- **markdown** `undefined | false | true`\n  Force markdown on/off. Defaults to on for assistant, off for user.\n- **prose-size** `undefined | \"xs\" | \"sm\" | \"base\" | \"lg\"`\n  Text/markdown sizing for the message body.\n- **code-theme** `undefined | string`\n  Shiki theme name used for fenced code blocks in the content.\n- **code-highlight** `undefined | false | true`\n  Disable syntax highlighting for code blocks (no Shiki loads).\n- **actions-reveal** `undefined | \"always\" | \"hover\"`\n  Whether the action bar is always visible (`'always'`, default) or only revealed on hover of the message row (`'hover'`).\n- **avatar-src** `undefined | string`\n  Convenience avatar image URL (used when `message.avatar` is not set).\n- **avatar-fallback** `undefined | string`\n  Convenience avatar fallback text (used when `message.avatar` is not set).\n- **avatar** `undefined | string`\n  Avatar rail mode. `'none'` omits the avatar rail entirely so the body spans the full row (predictable layout when you never show avatars). Any other value keeps the default behaviour: the built-in avatar when one resolves, or your `slot=\"avatar\"` content when projected (which REPLACES the built-in).\n\n### Events (CustomEvent, listen via addEventListener)\n- **kai-message-action** — An action button was clicked. `action` is the built-in name or custom id. `state` is present only for the toggleable feedback votes: `'on'` when a like/dislike is set, `'off'` when re-tapped to clear. `detail`: `{ messageId: string; action: string; state?: undefined | \"on\" | \"off\" }`\n\n### Methods (call these on the element instance)\nGet the element (`const el = document.querySelector('kai-message')`), then call it. Methods drive the element from your code; the events above report back what the user did. Both are public API.\n- **copy(): void** — Copy the message content to the clipboard and show the copied check.\n\n### Composition slots\nProject your own markup into these named regions — add a light-DOM child with a `slot=\"…\"` attribute (e.g. `<div slot=\"sidebar\">…</div>`).\n- **before-body** — A per-message header at the TOP of the body, above reasoning/tools/content: a model-name label, a role + timestamp line.\n- **after-body** — A row at the BOTTOM of the body, below the action bar: a citation/sources row, a token-cost/latency line.\n- **avatar** — Replaces the built-in avatar rail with your own node. Use `avatar=\"none\"` to omit the rail and let the body span the full row.\n\n### Styleable parts (`::part`)\nRestyle these from outside the Shadow DOM via `kai-message::part(name) { … }`.\n- **row** — The message row wrapper (avatar rail + body column). Restyle its gap or alignment from outside.\n  ```css\n  kai-message::part(row) { gap: 0.75rem }\n  ```\n- **bubble** — The content bubble wrapper. Restyle its background, radius, or padding; for a user message this is the rounded chat bubble.\n  ```css\n  kai-message::part(bubble) { background: var(--color-primary); color: var(--color-primary-foreground) }\n  ```\n- **content** — The rendered message text/markdown region (same node as `bubble`). Target it to tune typography from outside.\n  ```css\n  kai-message::part(content) { font-size: 0.9375rem }\n  ```\n- **actions** — The action-bar row (copy / like / regenerate …). Restyle its spacing or hide it entirely from outside.\n  ```css\n  kai-message::part(actions) { gap: 0.25rem }\n  ```\n- **citations** — The citation row rendered from the message’s `source` parts: a wrapped row of chips below the bubble, never inside it. Restyle its spacing or hide it entirely from outside.\n  ```css\n  kai-message::part(citations) { gap: 0.5rem }\n  ```\n- **attachment** — One attachment item: the chip, row or tile, whichever variant is rendering. Restyle its background, radius or border from outside without caring which layout it is.\n  ```css\n  kai-chat::part(attachment) { border-radius: 0.25rem }\n  ```\n- **attachment-name** — The attachment’s filename label. Present in every variant that shows one (a grid tile omits it for an image, which is its own label). Retune its type or hide it entirely.\n  ```css\n  kai-chat::part(attachment-name) { font-size: 0.75rem }\n  ```\n- **avatar** — Replaces the built-in avatar rail with your own node. Use `avatar=\"none\"` to omit the rail and let the body span the full row.\n\n### Invariants\nRules that have already broken real consumers of this kit. Each block says what enforces it — read that line rather than assuming CI catches a violation, because nothing here reads YOUR code. 3 of the 6 below are enforced by NOTHING at all, and 2 more by only half of what they say. Diagnosis and wrong/right examples for every invariant are served once, not repeated per element — call component_reference with { name: \"invariants\" }.\n\n#### props-not-attributes — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nSet arrays, objects and functions as JS PROPERTIES on the element instance. Only scalars (strings, numbers, booleans) belong in attributes, and the derived layer's scalar flag records which prop is which. What actually goes wrong, because the mechanism is not the obvious one: a framework template binding or String() stringifies an array to '[object Object]', which is not JSON, and the attribute path falls back to handing the element that raw STRING — so the prop is silently a string and the list renders nothing. A function cannot survive JSON at all: JSON.stringify({ onSubmit }) is '{}', so every callback is dropped. And an attribute re-set is not how updates are delivered — see reactivity-two-halves. A hand-written, valid-JSON attribute does happen to parse today, because a transitive dependency JSON.parses attributes whose declared default is not a string, but that is that dependency's behaviour and not this kit's contract; do not build on it. NOTHING IN THIS REPO ENFORCES THIS — it is a consumer contract, and the scalar flag in the derived layer is how the catalog SERVES the fact, which is not the same as checking it.\n\n#### events-non-bubbling — enforced by a structural guarantee in packages/ui/src/elements/define.tsx\nNon-bubbling is the default: public kai-* events are dispatched through the one helper that hard-codes bubbles:false and composed:false, so listen on the element itself, never on a parent or document. The protocol exceptions (kai-maximize-intent, kai-maximize-state and kai-card) bubble or compose deliberately and are listed in the derived layer under eventExceptions — do not generalise from them to the rest.\n\n#### host-coordinates — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nThere is no store. Data flows in via properties, out via events, and the host wires element A to element B. Solid context does not cross element boundaries, so nothing coordinates elements except the host application, and no element owns another. They run in the required test job, so the vectors they pin cannot come back — but NOTHING structural stops a NEW sink landing unguarded. No lint script in the package is about sinks, and the coupling map's unenforced list has no entry for the class. If you skip registering it the element never upgrades: nothing renders, **no error and no warning is logged**, a property you set assigns and reads back correctly, and `customElements.whenDefined('kai-message')` never resolves.\n\n```ts\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements/message'; // or just this one\n```\n\nTypeScript: `import type { KaiMessageElement } from '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';` — the element interface ships with the package; do not hand-roll a structural type.\n\n### AI/UI contract\n`kai-*` elements accept **array and object data as JavaScript properties** (set in JavaScript via `el.property = value`, not as HTML attributes). Events are native CustomEvents — listen with `el.addEventListener(\"event-name\", handler)` and read `event.detail` for the payload.\n\n### Card contract\n`<kai-message>` renders generative-UI cards inside the thread. It is a HOST: it draws whatever envelopes arrive, and carries the two props that say what a card may be.\n\n- **`cardTypes`** — `Record<envelope type, custom element tag>`. What DRAWS a card. Merged over the built-ins, so your entry overrides ours and the other 6 still render.\n- **`cardSchemas`** — `Record<envelope type, JSON Schema>`. What a VALID card looks like. This is the prop that carries YOUR OWN card schemas into the browser validator; the 7 built-ins are already known. Both are objects, so both are JS properties (`el.cardSchemas = …`), never HTML attributes.\n\nWrite both once with `createCardRegistry({ use, custom })` from `@kitn.ai/ui/schemas` and thread it to both ends: `el.cardTypes = cards.tags` / `el.cardSchemas = cards.validationSchemas` on the client, `cardTools(cards, { provider })` on the route.\n\nBuilt-in card types (ask `component_reference` for one of these tags to get its schema and tool definition):\n- `artifact` → `<kai-artifact>`, tool `kai_artifact`\n- `choice` → `<kai-choice>`, tool `kai_choice`\n- `confirm` → `<kai-confirm>`, tool `kai_confirm`\n- `embed` → `<kai-embed>`, tool `kai_embed`\n- `form` → `<kai-form>`, tool `kai_form`\n- `link` → `<kai-link-preview>`, tool `kai_link`\n- `tasks` → `<kai-tasks>`, tool `kai_tasks`\n\n#### Wiring the loop\n- **Tool names are `kai_<card type>`.** `kai_artifact` produces `{ type: \"artifact\" }`. Use `isCardTool(name)` to split card calls from your own tools; never match the prefix by hand.\n- **`tool_call_id` becomes `CardEnvelope.id`, unchanged.** That is what makes a revision UPSERT: a model that re-sends the same tool call id replaces the card in place instead of rendering a second copy of it. Generating your own id breaks that silently.\n- **`cardFromToolCall(name, input, { id: call.id })`** turns the call into a renderable envelope, or returns `null` when it is one of your own tools so the loop falls through to it. It never throws.\n- **Everything above is one import:** `import { cardTools, cardFromToolCall, isCardTool } from '@kitn.ai/ui/schemas'`. The raw documents also ship as JSON (`@kitn.ai/ui/schemas/artifact.schema.json`) for a Python or Go backend.\n\n### Props (JavaScript properties)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **message** `undefined | { id: string; tooltip?: undefined | string })[]; avatar?: undefined | { src?: undefined | string; fallback?: undefined | string; alt?: undefined | string }; feedback?: undefined | \"like\" | \"dislike\" }` ⚑ set as a JS property, not an HTML attribute\n  The full message object. Set as a JS property.\n- **role** `undefined | \"user\" | \"assistant\"`\n  Who is speaking: `'user'` or `'assistant'`. Convenience for simple cases when not passing a `message` object. This is the SEMANTIC role of the message, not an ARIA role. The name collides with the global ARIA `role` attribute, which is why the facade lifts it off the host (see `liftRoleOffHost`). Neither speaker is a valid ARIA role, so a `role=\"user\"` left on `<kai-message>` is a CRITICAL axe `aria-roles` violation. The accessible role lives on the row inside the shadow root instead: `role=\"article\"` plus an `aria-label` naming the speaker, matching the SolidJS `<Message>` component.\n- **markdown** `undefined | false | true`\n  Force markdown on/off. Defaults to on for assistant, off for user.\n- **proseSize** `undefined | \"xs\" | \"sm\" | \"base\" | \"lg\"`\n  Text/markdown sizing for the message body.\n- **codeTheme** `undefined | string`\n  Shiki theme name used for fenced code blocks in the content.\n- **codeHighlight** `undefined | false | true`\n  Disable syntax highlighting for code blocks (no Shiki loads).\n- **actionsReveal** `undefined | \"always\" | \"hover\"`\n  Whether the action bar is always visible (`'always'`, default) or only revealed on hover of the message row (`'hover'`).\n- **avatarSrc** `undefined | string`\n  Convenience avatar image URL (used when `message.avatar` is not set).\n- **avatarFallback** `undefined | string`\n  Convenience avatar fallback text (used when `message.avatar` is not set).\n- **avatar** `undefined | string`\n  Avatar rail mode. `'none'` omits the avatar rail entirely so the body spans the full row (predictable layout when you never show avatars). Any other value keeps the default behaviour: the built-in avatar when one resolves, or your `slot=\"avatar\"` content when projected (which REPLACES the built-in).\n- **cardTypes** `undefined | Record<string, string>` ⚑ set as a JS property, not an HTML attribute\n  Optional card type -> custom-element tag overrides/additions for `card` parts (merged over the built-ins). Property: `el.cardTypes`. Typed as a plain string map (not the `CardTagMap` alias) so the generated React wrapper inlines it instead of emitting an unresolved named type.\n- **cardSchemas** `undefined | Record<string, object>` ⚑ set as a JS property, not an HTML attribute\n  JSON Schemas for the card types this app renders, keyed by envelope type. The companion of `cardTypes`, which says what DRAWS a card while this says what a VALID one looks like. An OBJECT, so it is a JS property only: `el.cardSchemas = { 'pricing-table': pricingSchema }`, never an attribute. `createCardRegistry(...).validationSchemas` is exactly this shape. Without it the kit validates its own seven built-ins and leaves your own card type, the one your app actually cares about, as the only unchecked thing on screen. A schema here WINS over a built-in of the same name. Typed `Record<string, object>` rather than `Record<string, JsonSchema>` deliberately: an imported `.json` schema widens `\"type\"` to `string`, and an authored one carries `$schema`/`title`/`description`/`additionalProperties`, so the tighter type would reject both of the normal ways to supply one.\n\n### Attributes (HTML-safe)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **role** `undefined | \"user\" | \"assistant\"`\n  Who is speaking: `'user'` or `'assistant'`. Methods drive the element from your code; the events above report back what the user did. Both are public API.\n- **copy(): void** — Copy the message content to the clipboard and show the copied check.\n\n### Composition slots\nProject your own markup into these named regions — add a light-DOM child with a `slot=\"…\"` attribute (e.g. `<div slot=\"sidebar\">…</div>`).\n- **before-body** — A per-message header at the TOP of the body, above reasoning/tools/content: a model-name label, a role + timestamp line.\n- **after-body** — A row at the BOTTOM of the body, below the action bar: a citation/sources row, a token-cost/latency line.\n- **avatar** — Replaces the built-in avatar rail with your own node. Use `avatar=\"none\"` to omit the rail and let the body span the full row.\n\n### Styleable parts (`::part`)\nRestyle these from outside the Shadow DOM via `kai-message::part(name) { … }`.\n- **row** — The message row wrapper (avatar rail + body column). Restyle its gap or alignment from outside.\n  ```css\n  kai-message::part(row) { gap: 0.75rem }\n  ```\n- **bubble** — The content bubble wrapper. Restyle its background, radius, or padding; for a user message this is the rounded chat bubble.\n  ```css\n  kai-message::part(bubble) { background: var(--color-primary); color: var(--color-primary-foreground) }\n  ```\n- **content** — The rendered message text/markdown region (same node as `bubble`). Target it to tune typography from outside.\n  ```css\n  kai-message::part(content) { font-size: 0.9375rem }\n  ```\n- **actions** — The action-bar row (copy / like / regenerate …). Restyle its spacing or hide it entirely from outside.\n  ```css\n  kai-message::part(actions) { gap: 0.25rem }\n  ```\n- **citations** — The citation row rendered from the message’s `source` parts: a wrapped row of chips below the bubble, never inside it. Restyle its spacing or hide it entirely from outside.\n  ```css\n  kai-message::part(citations) { gap: 0.5rem }\n  ```\n- **attachment** — One attachment item: the chip, row or tile, whichever variant is rendering. Restyle its background, radius or border from outside without caring which layout it is.\n  ```css\n  kai-chat::part(attachment) { border-radius: 0.25rem }\n  ```\n- **attachment-name** — The attachment’s filename label. Present in every variant that shows one (a grid tile omits it for an image, which is its own label). Retune its type or hide it entirely.\n  ```css\n  kai-chat::part(attachment-name) { font-size: 0.75rem }\n  ```\n- **avatar** — Replaces the built-in avatar rail with your own node. Use `avatar=\"none\"` to omit the rail and let the body span the full row.\n\n### Invariants\nRules that have already broken real consumers of this kit. Each block says what enforces it — read that line rather than assuming CI catches a violation, because nothing here reads YOUR code. 3 of the 6 below are enforced by NOTHING at all, and 2 more by only half of what they say. Diagnosis and wrong/right examples for every invariant are served once, not repeated per element — call component_reference with { name: \"invariants\" }.\n\n#### props-not-attributes — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nSet arrays, objects and functions as JS PROPERTIES on the element instance. Only scalars (strings, numbers, booleans) belong in attributes, and the derived layer's scalar flag records which prop is which. What actually goes wrong, because the mechanism is not the obvious one: a framework template binding or String() stringifies an array to '[object Object]', which is not JSON, and the attribute path falls back to handing the element that raw STRING — so the prop is silently a string and the list renders nothing. A function cannot survive JSON at all: JSON.stringify({ onSubmit }) is '{}', so every callback is dropped. And an attribute re-set is not how updates are delivered — see reactivity-two-halves. A hand-written, valid-JSON attribute does happen to parse today, because a transitive dependency JSON.parses attributes whose declared default is not a string, but that is that dependency's behaviour and not this kit's contract; do not build on it. NOTHING IN THIS REPO ENFORCES THIS — it is a consumer contract, and the scalar flag in the derived layer is how the catalog SERVES the fact, which is not the same as checking it.\n\n#### events-non-bubbling — enforced by a structural guarantee in packages/ui/src/elements/define.tsx\nNon-bubbling is the default: public kai-* events are dispatched through the one helper that hard-codes bubbles:false and composed:false, so listen on the element itself, never on a parent or document. The protocol exceptions (kai-maximize-intent, kai-maximize-state and kai-card) bubble or compose deliberately and are listed in the derived layer under eventExceptions — do not generalise from them to the rest.\n\n#### host-coordinates — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nThere is no store. Data flows in via properties, out via events, and the host wires element A to element B. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01A6XfTffEUKf8cTKfi9msMm","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"# AI/UI scaffold — chat + tool + attachments × Mock (local preview)\ncombo: kai-chat+kai-tool+kai-attachments × mock × full-page × html\nstream: No backend and no provider, but a real wire. createMockResponder() from @kitn.ai/ui/state yields canned SSE frames and the scaffold reads them with readOpenAIStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire, on the same code path a real route's response takes: same reader, same part folding, same abort handling. The frames carry the OpenAI chat-completions shape because the mock stands in for your /api/chat ROUTE, not for a provider (every other integration here re-frames its provider to that shape server-side), so going live replaces ONE expression: mockResponse(value) becomes the awaited response from your own chat route, and nothing else in the handler changes. Nothing here can be mistaken for a real turn: the stream opens with a ': kai-mock' SSE comment, every frame carries a _kai_mock field naming createMockResponder, model reports as 'kai-mock' (no provider serves it), and usage is all zeros. Block (2) serves the same frames over HTTP as an OPTIONAL route, so the /api/chat seam can be stood up before any provider exists.\n\n=== (1) FRONT-END (html, fills the viewport (fixed, inset 0)) ===\n\n<!-- index.html — paste this into <body>. -->\n<!-- chat + tool + attachments — fills the viewport (fixed, inset 0) -->\n<!-- FULL PAGE MEANS FULL PAGE: `position: fixed; inset: 0` is deliberate, not a stray overlay. -->\n<!-- `height: 100dvh` is full-page only in an empty document, and stock starters are not empty — -->\n<!-- Vite's react-ts template caps #root at 1126px, centres its text and border-boxes it, and the -->\n<!-- TanStack Start starter wraps every route in a Header + Footer that pushed the composer 13px -->\n<!-- below the fold at 1280x800. Fixed positioning escapes both, `text-align: start` undoes the -->\n<!-- inherited centring, and z-index keeps a sticky header from painting over the thread. -->\n<!-- The z-index stays BELOW 100: the kit's kai-toast-region (a body-level sibling toast() mounts) paints at z-index var(--kai-toast-z, 100), so a wrapper at or above it buries every toast. -->\n<!-- This DOES cover the chrome around it (nav included) — that is what full-page means here. -->\n<!-- Want the chat to sit INSIDE your own layout instead? Use placement: \"inline\". -->\n<div style=\"position: fixed; inset: 0; display: flex; flex-direction: column; text-align: start; z-index: 90;\">\n  <kai-chat id=\"chat\" suggestion-mode=\"submit\" style=\"flex: 1; min-height: 0;\"></kai-chat>\n  <!-- kai-tool / kai-reasoning render INSIDE the thread, not as siblings: they are\n       parts on a message — parts: [{ type: 'reasoning', … }, { type: 'tool', tool: {…} }, …]\n       — and the stream in the script below builds them as the model works. -->\n  <kai-attachments></kai-attachments>\n  <!-- wire data props — see the component_reference MCP tool -->\n</div>\n<!-- The logic is a real module, NOT an inline script: a stock vanilla-ts\n     tsconfig is \"include\": [\"src\"], so anything inline is type-checked by\n     nothing at all. Delete the template's src/main.ts and save the file\n     below in its place — this tag is the one the template already ships. -->\n<script type=\"module\" src=\"/src/main.ts\"></script>\n\n<!-- empty-state hint: add an empty-state prompt that fits your product -->\n\n// ── src/main.ts ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// src/main.ts — the page's logic, in a module YOUR build type-checks.\n//\n// It lives here rather than inline in index.html on purpose: the canonical\n// getting-started path (`npm create vite -- --template vanilla-ts`) builds with\n// `tsc && vite build` and scopes its tsconfig to \"include\": [\"src\"], so an\n// inline <script> is checked by nothing at all. Delete the template's own\n// src/main.ts and save this in its place; index.html already points at it.\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';  // registers <kai-*> — required, must come first\n// The kit ships the element interfaces, so one cast at the lookup below types\n// every property assignment that follows.\nimport type { KaiChatElement } from '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';\nimport { createAssistantStream, createMockResponder, type ChatMessage } from '@kitn.ai/ui/state';\nimport { readOpenAIStream } from '@kitn.ai/ui/wire';\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/theme.tokens.css';  // compiled token defaults; use theme.css only for Tailwind-source apps\n\n// The kit's own mock responder — no backend, no key, no network. It streams\n// canned SSE frames through the same reader a real provider's response uses,\n// and marks every one of them as a mock. Shared with create-kai and the\n// starters, so there is one implementation of this and not seven.\nconst mockResponse = createMockResponder();\n\nasync function init() {\n  const chat = document.getElementById('chat') as KaiChatElement;\n  // SCAF-15: kai-* register via an async dynamic import (SSR-safety), so the\n  // element may not be upgraded yet. Wait for the upgrade before setting any\n  // array/object property — values set pre-upgrade are dropped on upgrade.\n  await customElements.whenDefined('kai-chat');\n  // suggestions is a JS PROPERTY (arrays can't be HTML attributes)\n  chat.suggestions = [\"What's new?\", \"How can you help?\"];\n  chat.suggestionMode = 'submit';\n\n  // Tool calls and reasoning render INSIDE the thread, as parts on the\n  // assistant message — the stream in onSubmit builds them as the model works.\n  // The thread starts EMPTY so the suggestions show and turn one carries no\n  // conversation the user never had.\n  // This local preview has no provider to send it to, so here — and only\n  // here — you can uncomment a fixture to see a filled panel with no backend\n  // (replace the empty initializer below with it):\n  // chat.messages = [{\"id\":\"sample-assistant\",\"role\":\"assistant\",\"parts\":[{\"type\":\"reasoning\",\"text\":\"I should call the search tool to get up-to-date data.\"},{\"type\":\"tool\",\"tool\":{\"type\":\"search\",\"state\":\"output-available\",\"input\":{\"query\":\"current pricing\"},\"output\":{\"results\":[\"Result A\",\"Result B\"]},\"toolCallId\":\"tc_001\"}},{\"type\":\"text\",\"text\":\"Searched the web for current pricing.\"}]}];\n\n  // messages is a JS PROPERTY (objects can't be HTML attributes)\n  chat.addEventListener('kai-submit', async (event: Event) => {\n    const e = event as CustomEvent<{ value: string }>;\n    const value = e.detail.value.trim();\n    if (!value) return;\n    const history: ChatMessage[] = [...chat.messages, { id: crypto.randomUUID(), role: 'user' as const, parts: [{ type: 'text', text: value }] }];\n    chat.messages = history;\n    chat.loading = true;\n    // createAssistantStream appends the in-flight assistant message and folds\n    // every delta onto its parts. readOpenAIStream parses the SSE: keep-alive\n    // comments, multi-line frames, split codepoints, tool calls, reasoning.\n    const stream = createAssistantStream((fn) => { chat.messages = fn(chat.messages); });\n    try {\n      // NO BACKEND AND NO PROVIDER. mockResponse() returns canned SSE frames that\n      // are read by the SAME parser a real model's response goes through, so this\n      // preview exercises the real path. Every frame is marked as a mock (a\n      // ': kai-mock' banner, a _kai_mock field, model 'kai-mock', zero usage), so\n      // nothing here can be mistaken for a real turn.\n      //\n      // TO GO LIVE, only this one line changes: `res` becomes the POST to your\n      // route, with toOpenAIMessages(history) as the body. Rather than copy\n      // that request into a comment here — where it would drift from the real\n      // one — scaffold again with a provider (integration: 'openrouter', 'ollama',\n      // …) and the emitted code is the exact replacement, backend route included.\n      // Everything below this line is already the real path and stays as it is.\n      const res = mockResponse(value);\n      const turn = await readOpenAIStream(res, stream);\n      if (turn.error) console.error('Model error:', turn.error.message);\n    } catch (err) {\n      // Without this a bad key is a permanently blank assistant bubble plus an\n      // unhandled rejection. abort(reason) settles the message AND puts the\n      // reason where the reader can see it: onto any tool panel still waiting\n      // on a result (output-error, so nothing spins forever), or — on a\n      // text-only turn, which has no panel to carry it — as a new text part in\n      // the thread. Text that already streamed stays put.\n      // The reason is SHOWN TO THE USER. err.message is the honest default;\n      // swap in a sentence a visitor can read once you know your failure modes.\n      // The && is load-bearing: an Error is free to carry an empty message, and\n      // an empty reason is nothing to show — back to the blank bubble.\n      stream.abort(err instanceof Error && err.message ? err.message : 'Request failed');\n      console.error(err);  // the console is for you; the thread is for them\n    } finally {\n      // done() SETTLES the message: every sink call after it is dropped, which\n      // is why the whole loop runs above it and not after.\n      stream.done();\n      chat.loading = false;\n    }\n  });\n}\n\n// A <script type=\"module\"> is deferred, so the DOM is already parsed here.\nvoid init();\n\n=== (2) BACKEND ROUTE ===\n\n# OPTIONAL — the front end above streams its reply locally, so this scaffold\n# RUNS with no backend and no key. The route below is the mock's server half:\n# the same createMockResponder() frames served over HTTP, so you can stand up\n# the real /api/chat seam before any provider exists. To use it, swap the\n# onSubmit's `const res = mockResponse(value);` for a request to /api/chat and\n# hand its response body to the same reader. Going live later then means\n# editing THIS file only — replace the responder with a provider call, or\n# re-scaffold with a real integration and this block becomes its route.\n# Note: Mock (local preview) has no route for \"html\". Emitting its native\n# Express handler (Node) route instead (matches the integration's ts language).\n#\n# WARNING: the route below is written for Express handler (Node) and will NOT run\n# as-is in a static HTML page.\n#   • or ignore it: the front end above already streams the mock locally, so\n#     nothing in this scaffold needs the route to run.\n#   • a static page has no server at all — run this route on a separate server (framework: \"express\" | \"worker\" | \"next\") and either proxy /api/chat to it or point the fetch at its absolute URL (CORS applies).\n#   • or run it where it belongs: framework: \"express\".\n\n// server.ts\nimport type { OpenAIWireMessage } from '@kitn.ai/ui/wire';\nimport express from 'express';\n\nimport { createMockResponder } from '@kitn.ai/ui/state';\n\n// The kit's own mock responder — no provider, no key, no upstream. MODULE\n// scope, not per-request: the responder owns the cursor into its canned\n// replies, so rebuilding it per turn would answer with the first one forever.\nconst mockResponse = createMockResponder();\n\n/**\n * What the front end POSTs. `request.json()` is `unknown` (it is whatever the\n * client sent), so the body is narrowed once here instead of at every use —\n * without it this route does not compile under a server tsconfig. Widen it as\n * you add fields of your own.\n */\ntype ChatRequestBody = {\n  messages: OpenAIWireMessage[];\n  model?: string;\n  tools?: unknown[];\n};\n\nclass ChatRequestError extends Error {\n  constructor(readonly status: number, message: string) { super(message); }\n}\n\n/** Narrow the JSON body once, at the edge. A bare GET, a malformed body, or a\n *  missing messages array is a ChatRequestError with a status — NEVER an\n *  unhandled SyntaxError: one killed a Vite dev server (findings F-10). */\nasync function readChatRequest(request: Request): Promise<ChatRequestBody> {\n  if (request.method !== 'POST') {\n    throw new ChatRequestError(405, `Method ${request.method} not allowed — POST /api/chat.`);\n  }\n  let parsed: unknown;\n  try { parsed = await request.json(); } catch {\n    throw new ChatRequestError(400, 'Request body is not valid JSON.');\n  }\n  const body = parsed as ChatRequestBody;\n  if (!Array.isArray(body?.messages)) {\n    throw new ChatRequestError(400, 'Request body must carry a messages array.');\n  }\n  return body;\n}\n\n/** Map a guard rejection to the Response its status demands; rethrow anything\n *  else — an unexpected error should be loud, not laundered into a 400. */\nfunction toChatErrorResponse(error: unknown): Response {\n  if (error instanceof ChatRequestError) return Response.json({ error: error.message }, { status: error.status });\n  throw error;\n}\n\nasync function chatHandler(request: Request): Promise<Response> {\n  // The responder cycles its replies whatever you send, so the prompt is a\n  // courtesy — but the body is still read exactly the way a real route reads\n  // it, so swapping this handler for a provider's changes nothing upstream of\n  // this file.\n  let chatBody: ChatRequestBody;\n  try {\n    chatBody = await readChatRequest(request);\n  } catch (error) {\n    return toChatErrorResponse(error);\n  }\n  const { messages } = chatBody;\n  const last = [...messages].reverse().find((m) => m.role === 'user');\n  const prompt = last && typeof last.content === 'string' ? last.content : '';\n\n  // createMockResponder() yields COMPLETE OpenAI chat-completions SSE frames —\n  // the same strings the front end's local preview streams — so this route only\n  // writes them out verbatim. No framing is built here; the browser parses them\n  // with readOpenAIStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire, exactly as it would a real\n  // route's response. Every frame is marked as a mock (a ': kai-mock' banner, a\n  // _kai_mock field, model 'kai-mock', zero usage).\n  const encoder = new TextEncoder();\n  let open = true;\n  const body = new ReadableStream<Uint8Array>({\n    async start(controller) {\n      for await (const frame of mockResponse(prompt)) {\n        // The browser hangs up when the user asks a new question mid-answer;\n        // stop producing frames rather than writing into a cancelled stream.\n        if (!open) return;\n        controller.enqueue(encoder.encode(frame));\n      }\n      controller.close();\n    },\n    cancel() {\n      open = false;\n    },\n  });\n\n  return new Response(body, {\n    status: 200,\n    headers: {\n      'Content-Type': 'text/event-stream; charset=utf-8',\n      // no-transform stops a proxy buffering the stream into one blob.\n      'Cache-Control': 'no-cache, no-transform',\n      Connection: 'keep-alive',\n      // A proxy in front of a preview build would otherwise buffer the whole\n      // stream and the reply would land all at once instead of streaming.\n      'X-Accel-Buffering': 'no',\n    },\n  });\n}\n\nconst app = express();\napp.use(express.json());\n\n// Express is (req, res) — it neither takes a Request nor returns a Response, so\n// the web handler is bridged here. Node 18+ has Request/Response as globals.\napp.post('/api/chat', async (req, res) => {\n  const response = await chatHandler(\n    new Request('http://localhost/api/chat', {\n      method: 'POST',\n      headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },\n      body: JSON.stringify(req.body),\n    }),\n  );\n\n  // The STATUS has to survive the bridge: a 401 from the provider that arrives\n  // at the browser as a 200 is a blank bubble and no error.\n  res.status(response.status);\n  // Annotated: a server-side tsconfig has no DOM lib, so Headers comes from\n  // @types/node and these params are implicitly `any` (TS7006) under noImplicitAny.\n  response.headers.forEach((value: string, key: string) => res.setHeader(key, value));\n  if (!response.body) { res.end(); return; }\n\n  // Write each chunk as it lands — buffering here defeats streaming.\n  const reader = response.body.getReader();\n  for (;;) {\n    const { value, done } = await reader.read();\n    if (done) break;\n    res.write(value);\n  }\n  res.end();\n});\n\napp.listen(3001, () => console.log('chat api: http://localhost:3001/api/chat'));\n// The front end fetches a RELATIVE /api/chat, so proxy that path to this port\n// from your dev server, or serve both from one origin.\n\n=== (3) RUN NOTE ===\n\nNo backend or API key needed: the reply is generated in the browser and parsed by the same reader a real route feeds. Run the front-end as-is; swap `integration` for a real provider (openai, anthropic, openrouter, ollama) when ready, and the emitted handler differs by one expression. Block (2) is the OPTIONAL server half: the same mock frames served over HTTP, for standing up the /api/chat seam before any provider exists.\n\nInstall:\n  npm install @kitn.ai/ui\n\nEnv vars to set:\n  (none)\n\nKey handling: frontend-safe — no server hop is required.\n  Nothing here is secret and nothing needs a capability a browser lacks, so the\n  page may talk to this integration itself.\n  The route in block (2) is a convenience — one origin, one place to log — and\n  not a requirement.\n\n=== LOADING OPTIONS ===\n\nThe scaffold uses `import '@kitn.ai/ui/elements'` (register-all) — the right\ndefault: it registers every kai-* element and is SSR-safe, so leave it as is.\nTwo opt-in modes load less if a page only ever uses a few elements:\n\n  Per-element (bundler apps): import '@kitn.ai/ui/elements/<file>'\n    Registers just that element; your bundler tree-shakes the rest away.\n    Example: import '@kitn.ai/ui/elements/chat'  (client-only — not for SSR)\n\n  Autoloader (no-build / CDN pages): a <script type=\"module\"> tag pointing at\n    dist/elements/autoloader.js — loads each kai-* element on demand as it\n    appears in the DOM. A CDN/static-file tool; not importable through a bundler.\n\nRun the debug tool with \"reduce bundle size\" for the full breakdown and sizes.\n\n=== INTERACTION PATTERNS ===\n\nOptional snippets for confirmations, card recovery, preference capture, and state/streaming helpers.\nDrop in the one(s) you need; all imports resolve from @kitn.ai/ui.\n\n--- Pattern: toast() — confirmation + Undo ---\n// toast is IMPERATIVE — call it; there is no <kai-toast> to place. The first\n// call auto-mounts one <kai-toast-region> on document.body. Exported from\n// both '@kitn.ai/ui' and '@kitn.ai/ui/elements'.\nimport { toast } from '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';\n\ntoast('Copied to clipboard');      // neutral, auto-dismisses\ntoast.success('Saved');            // emerald success variant\n\n// Undo affordance: an action floors the duration so there's time to act.\nconst t = toast('Item deleted', {\n  action: { label: 'Undo', onAction: () => restoreItem() },\n});\n// t.update({ message: 'Restored', variant: 'success' });  t.dismiss();\n\n// Collapsed (Sonner-style) stacking — toasts pile + expand on hover/focus.\n// Opt in once at startup, or per-region via <kai-toast-region stack=\"collapsed\">.\nimport { configureToasts } from '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';\nconfigureToasts({ stack: 'collapsed' });\n\n--- Pattern: dismissRecovery() — card dismiss + Undo (DEFERRED, not deleted) ---\n// Dismissing a generative-UI card does NOT delete its envelope — it stamps a\n// 'dismissed' resolution and collapses to a reopenable stub. Shared with create-kai and the\n// starters, so there is one implementation of this and not seven.\nconst mockResponse = createMockResponder();\n\nasync function init() {\n  const chat = document.getElementById('chat') as KaiChatElement;\n  // SCAF-15: kai-* register via an async dynamic import (SSR-safety), so the\n  // element may not be upgraded yet. history: ChatMessage[] = [...chat.messages, { id: crypto.randomUUID(), role: 'user' as const, parts: [{ type: 'text', text: value }] }];\n    chat.messages = history;\n    chat.loading = true;\n    // createAssistantStream appends the in-flight assistant message and folds\n    // every delta onto its parts. readOpenAIStream parses the SSE: keep-alive\n    // comments, multi-line frames, split Every frame is marked as a mock (a\n      // ': kai-mock' banner, a _kai_mock field, model 'kai-mock', zero usage), so\n      // nothing here can be mistaken for a real turn.\n      //\n      // TO GO LIVE, only this one line changes: `res` becomes the POST to your\n      // route, with toOpenAIMessages(history) as the body. Rather than copy\n      // that request into a comment here — where it would drift from the real\n      // one — scaffold again with a provider (integration: 'openrouter', 'ollama',\n      // …) and the emitted code is the exact replacement, backend route included.\n      // Everything below this line is already the real path and stays as it is.\n      const res = mockResponse(value);\n      const turn = await readOpenAIStream(res, stream);\n      if (turn.error) console.error('Model error:', turn.error.message);\n    } catch (err) {\n      // Without this a bad key is a permanently blank assistant bubble plus an\n      // unhandled rejection. abort(reason) settles the message AND puts the\n      // reason where the reader can see it: onto any tool panel still waiting\n      // on a result (output-error, so nothing spins forever), or — on a\n      // text-only turn, which has no panel to carry it — as a new text part in\n      // the thread. Text that already streamed stays put.\n      // The reason is SHOWN TO THE USER. err.message is the honest default;\n      // swap in a sentence a visitor can read once you know your failure modes.\n      // The && is load-bearing: an Error is free to carry an empty message, and\n      // an empty reason is nothing to show — back to the blank bubble.\n      stream.abort(err instanceof Error && err.message ? err.message : 'Request failed');\n      console.error(err);  // the console is for you; the thread is for them\n    } finally {\n      // done() SETTLES the message: every sink call after it is dropped, which\n      // is why the whole loop runs above it and not after.\n      stream.done();\n      chat.loading = false;\n    }\n  });\n}\n\n// A <script type=\"module\"> is deferred, so the DOM is already parsed here.\nvoid init();\n\n=== (2) BACKEND ROUTE ===\n\n# OPTIONAL — the front end above streams its reply locally, so this scaffold\n# RUNS with no backend and no key. The route below is the mock's server half:\n# the same createMockResponder() frames served over HTTP, so you can stand up\n# the real /api/chat seam before any provider exists. To use it, swap the\n# onSubmit's `const res = mockResponse(value);` for a request to /api/chat and\n# hand its response body to the same reader. Going live later then means\n# editing THIS file only — replace the responder with a provider call, or\n# re-scaffold with a real integration and this block becomes its route.\n# Note: Mock (local preview) has no route for \"html\". Emitting its native\n# Express handler (Node) route instead (matches the integration's ts language).\n#\n# WARNING: the route below is written for Express handler (Node) and will NOT run\n# as-is in a static HTML page.\n#   • or ignore it: the front end above already streams the mock locally, so\n#     nothing in this scaffold needs the route to run.\n#   • a static page has no server at all — run this route on a separate server (framework: \"express\" | \"worker\" | \"next\") and either proxy /api/chat to it or point the fetch at its absolute URL (CORS applies).\n#   • or run it where it belongs: framework: \"express\".\n\n// server.ts\nimport type { OpenAIWireMessage } from '@kitn.ai/ui/wire';\nimport express from 'express';\n\nimport { createMockResponder } from '@kitn.ai/ui/state';\n\n// The kit's own mock responder — no provider, no key, no upstream. MODULE\n// scope, not per-request: the responder owns the cursor into its canned\n// replies, so rebuilding it per turn would answer with the first one forever.\nconst mockResponse = createMockResponder();\n\n/**\n * What the front end POSTs. `request.json()` is `unknown` (it is whatever the\n * client sent), so the body is narrowed once here instead of at every use —\n * without it this route does not compile under a server tsconfig. Widen it as\n * you add fields of your own.\n */\ntype ChatRequestBody = {\n  messages: OpenAIWireMessage[];\n  model?: string;\n  tools?: unknown[];\n};\n\nclass ChatRequestError extends Error {\n  constructor(readonly status: number, message: string) { super(message); }\n}\n\n/** Narrow the JSON body once, at the edge. A bare GET, a malformed body, or a\n *  missing messages array is a ChatRequestError with a status — NEVER an\n *  unhandled SyntaxError: one killed a Vite dev server (findings F-10). */\nasync function readChatRequest(request: Request): Promise<ChatRequestBody> {\n  if (request.method !== 'POST') {\n    throw new ChatRequestError(405, `Method ${request.method} not allowed — POST /api/chat.`);\n  }\n  let parsed: unknown;\n  try { parsed = await request.json(); } catch {\n    throw new ChatRequestError(400, 'Request body is not valid JSON.');\n  }\n  const body = parsed as ChatRequestBody;\n  if (!Array.isArray(body?.messages)) {\n    throw new ChatRequestError(400, 'Request body must carry a messages array.');\n  }\n  return body;\n}\n\n/** Map a guard rejection to the Response its status demands; rethrow anything\n *  else — an unexpected error should be loud, not laundered into a 400. */\nfunction toChatErrorResponse(error: unknown): Response {\n  if (error instanceof ChatRequestError) return Response.json({ error: error.message }, { status: error.status });\n  throw error;\n}\n\nasync function chatHandler(request: Request): Promise<Response> {\n  // The responder cycles its replies whatever you send, so the prompt is a\n  // courtesy — but the body is still read exactly the way a real route reads\n  // it, so swapping this handler for a provider's changes nothing upstream of\n  // this file.\n  let chatBody: ChatRequestBody;\n  try {\n    chatBody = await readChatRequest(request);\n  } catch (error) {\n    return toChatErrorResponse(error);\n  }\n  const { messages } = chatBody;\n  const last = [...messages].reverse().find((m) => m.role === 'user');\n  const prompt = last && typeof last.content === 'string' ? last.content : '';\n\n  // createMockResponder() yields COMPLETE OpenAI chat-completions SSE frames —\n  // the same strings the front end's local preview streams — so this route only\n  // writes them out verbatim. No framing is built here; the browser parses them\n  // with readOpenAIStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire, exactly as it would a real\n  // route's response. Every frame is marked as a mock (a ': kai-mock' banner, a\n  // _kai_mock field, model 'kai-mock', zero usage).\n  const encoder = new TextEncoder();\n  let open = true;\n  const body = new ReadableStream<Uint8Array>({\n    async start(controller) {\n      for await (const frame of mockResponse(prompt)) {\n        // The browser hangs up when the user asks a new question mid-answer;\n        // stop producing frames rather than writing into a cancelled stream.\n        if (!open) return;\n        controller.enqueue(encoder.encode(frame));\n      }\n      controller.close();\n    },\n    cancel() {\n      open = false;\n    },\n  });\n\n  return new Response(body, {\n    status: 200,\n    headers: {\n      'Content-Type': 'text/event-stream; charset=utf-8',\n      // no-transform stops a proxy buffering the stream into one blob.\n      'Cache-Control': 'no-cache, no-transform',\n      Connection: 'keep-alive',\n      // A proxy in front of a preview build would otherwise buffer the whole\n      // stream and the reply would land all at once instead of streaming.\n      'X-Accel-Buffering': 'no',\n    },\n  });\n}\n\nconst app = express();\napp.use(express.json());\n\n// Express is (req, res) — it neither takes a Request nor returns a Response, so\n// the web handler is bridged here. Node 18+ has Request/Response as globals.\napp.post('/api/chat', async (req, res) => {\n  const response = await chatHandler(\n    new Request('http://localhost/api/chat', {\n      method: 'POST',\n      headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },\n      body: JSON.stringify(req.body),\n    }),\n  );\n\n  // The STATUS has to survive the bridge: a 401 from the provider that arrives\n  // at the browser as a 200 is a blank bubble and no error.\n  res.status(response.status);\n  // Annotated: a server-side tsconfig has no DOM lib, so Headers comes from\n  // @types/node and these params are implicitly `any` (TS7006) under noImplicitAny.\n  response.headers.forEach((value: string, key: string) => res.setHeader(key, value));\n  if (!response.body) { res.end(); return; }\n\n  // Write each chunk as it lands — buffering here defeats streaming.\n  const reader = response.body.getReader();\n  for (;;) {\n    const { value, done } = await reader.read();\n    if (done) break;\n    res.write(value);\n  }\n  res.end();\n});\n\napp.listen(3001, () => console.log('chat api: http://localhost:3001/api/chat'));\n// The front end fetches a RELATIVE /api/chat, so proxy that path to this port\n// from your dev server, or serve both from one origin.\n\n=== (3) RUN NOTE ===\n\nNo backend or API key needed: the reply is generated in the browser and parsed by the same reader a real route feeds. If you skip registering it the element never upgrades: nothing renders, **no error and no warning is logged**, a property you set assigns and reads back correctly, and `customElements.whenDefined('kai-conversation-item')` never resolves.\n\n```ts\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements/conversation-item'; // or just this one\n```\n\nTypeScript: `import type { KaiConversationItemElement } from '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';` — the element interface ships with the package; do not hand-roll a structural type.\n\n### AI/UI contract\n`kai-*` elements accept **array and object data as JavaScript properties** (set in JavaScript via `el.property = value`, not as HTML attributes). Events are native CustomEvents — listen with `el.addEventListener(\"event-name\", handler)` and read `event.detail` for the payload.\n\n### Props (JavaScript properties)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **conversationId** `undefined | string`\n  The row's identity, handed to the container's selection contract. In the element this is the `conversation-id` attribute (host `id` is the fallback).\n- **active** `undefined | false | true`\n  Selected state. Reflected as `aria-current` on the row body and a `data-active` styling hook on the row; the container drives it from its `activeId`.\n- **compact** `undefined | false | true`\n  Dense single-line row padding.\n\n### Attributes (HTML-safe)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **conversation-id** `undefined | string`\n  The row's identity, handed to the container's selection contract. In the element this is the `conversation-id` attribute (host `id` is the fallback).\n- **active** `undefined | false | true`\n  Selected state. Reflected as `aria-current` on the row body and a `data-active` styling hook on the row; the container drives it from its `activeId`.\n- **compact** `undefined | false | true`\n  Dense single-line row padding.\n\n### Composition slots\nProject your own markup into these named regions — add a light-DOM child with a `slot=\"…\"` attribute (e.g. `<div slot=\"sidebar\">…</div>`).\n- **** — The row title. `leading`, `meta` and `menu` are the named regions around it.\n- **leading** — Leading region before the title (an icon or avatar).\n- **meta** — Meta region under the title (a timestamp or status line).\n- **menu** — Your own row menu (a popover trigger). Never selects the row.\n\n### Styleable parts (`::part`)\nRestyle these from outside the Shadow DOM via `kai-conversation-item::part(name) { … }`.\n- **body** — The activation surface inside the row (button role; carries aria-current and the roving tabindex). The focus ring paints here.\n  ```css\n  kai-conversation-item::part(body) { outline-offset: 2px }\n  ```\n- **row** — The whole row surface. Carries `data-active` while selected.\n  ```css\n  kai-conversation-item::part(row) { border-radius: 0.5rem }\n  ```\n- **title** — The title line (the default slot renders inside it).\n  ```css\n  kai-conversation-item::part(title) { font-weight: 600 }\n  ```\n- **leading** — Leading region before the title (an icon or avatar).\n- **meta** — Meta region under the title (a timestamp or status line).\n- **menu** — Your own row menu (a popover trigger). Never selects the row.\n\n### Invariants\nRules that have already broken real consumers of this kit. Each block says what enforces it — read that line rather than assuming CI catches a violation, because nothing here reads YOUR code. 3 of the 6 below are enforced by NOTHING at all, and 2 more by only half of what they say. Diagnosis and wrong/right examples for every invariant are served once, not repeated per element — call component_reference with { name: \"invariants\" }.\n\n#### props-not-attributes — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nSet arrays, objects and functions as JS PROPERTIES on the element instance. Only scalars (strings, numbers, booleans) belong in attributes, and the derived layer's scalar flag records which prop is which. What actually goes wrong, because the mechanism is not the obvious one: a framework template binding or String() stringifies an array to '[object Object]', which is not JSON, and the attribute path falls back to handing the element that raw STRING — so the prop is silently a string and the list renders nothing. A function cannot survive JSON at all: JSON.stringify({ onSubmit }) is '{}', so every callback is dropped. And an attribute re-set is not how updates are delivered — see reactivity-two-halves. A hand-written, valid-JSON attribute does happen to parse today, because a transitive dependency JSON.parses attributes whose declared default is not a string, but that is that dependency's behaviour and not this kit's contract; do not build on it. NOTHING IN THIS REPO ENFORCES THIS — it is a consumer contract, and the scalar flag in the derived layer is how the catalog SERVES the fact, which is not the same as checking it.\n\n#### events-non-bubbling — enforced by a structural guarantee in packages/ui/src/elements/define.tsx\nNon-bubbling is the default: public kai-* events are dispatched through the one helper that hard-codes bubbles:false and composed:false, so listen on the element itself, never on a parent or document. The protocol exceptions (kai-maximize-intent, kai-maximize-state and kai-card) bubble or compose deliberately and are listed in the derived layer under eventExceptions — do not generalise from them to the rest.\n\n#### host-coordinates — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nThere is no store. Data flows in via properties, out via events, and the host wires element A to element B. Solid context does not cross element boundaries, so nothing coordinates elements except the host application, and no element owns another. Placing two elements in the same subtree wires nothing.\n\n#### untrusted-model-output — PARTIALLY ENFORCED: one half is covered, by the kit's own tests (packages/ui/tests/components/markdown-xss.test.tsx, packages/ui/tests/components/artifact-url-xss.test.tsx, packages/ui/tests/components/hostile-model-output.test.tsx); the statement says which half is not\nEverything the model produced is untrusted input: a MessagePart, card envelope or tool argument reaching innerHTML, an href or src, window.open or an iframe is a vulnerability. THE DEFECT IS NEVER A MISSING GUARD, IT IS WHICH PATH GOT IT — every one found so far sat on a path the CONSUMER controls while the model-controlled path beside it had none. So put a policy on the sink, and MATCH THE LIST TO THE SINK rather than reaching for one universal list: http:, https: and mailto: for anything navigable the user may click, resolved against the page so ordinary relative links still work; http: and https: ONLY for a model-supplied citation, which is a reference to a page on the public web and has no business being relative. Those are two lists because there are two sinks — it is the same split the kit makes internally between SAFE_SCHEMES and RENDERABLE_SCHEMES, not a variant invented here. Whichever you use, parse inside a try/catch and RETURN FALSE on an unparseable URL: new URL() throws, and a throw at a sink crashes the render. And render model text as TEXT. Escaping is the correct rendering: the source text must stay VISIBLE as well as inert, because a filter that deleted it would pass the security check and be a worse UI. COVERAGE, and read this before trusting CI here: the three XSS suites are tests and ONLY tests. They run in the required test job, so the vectors they pin cannot come back — but NOTHING structural stops a NEW sink landing unguarded. No lint script in the package is about sinks, and the coupling map's unenforced list has no entry for the class. A new sink is caught in review or not at all.\n\n#### kit-parses-consumer-fetches — PARTIALLY ENFORCED: one half is covered, by the kit's `lint:silent-drops` script, in required CI; the statement says which half is not\nThe kit parses; the consumer fetches. Two halves, covered differently. KIT SIDE: every MessagePart variant the wire encodes must be accounted for, or a variant is gone once the request leaves — that half is enforced by lint:silent-drops in CI. CONSUMER SIDE: never hand-roll an SSE reader; import readOpenAIStream, readAnthropicStream or readModelStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire, and fetch from your own endpoint, because there is no client, no key handling and no provider SDK below wire/. NO CI CHECK COVERS THAT SECOND HALF — no guard reads consumer or scaffolded code for a hand-rolled reader; it is measured by the acceptance deck instead, at scenario S2, whose scoring line is 'imports readOpenAIStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire; no hand-rolled SSE reader anywhere in the output'.\n\n#### upgrade-race (script-tag delivery only) — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this, until issue #99 option B lands in defineWebComponent. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nA property set before the element upgrades is lost. On script-tag targets, load order is not ours. Until issue #99 option B (upgrade-property preservation in defineWebComponent) lands, every script-tag recipe must state this race loudly and set properties only after registration — await customElements.whenDefined(tag), which is the guarantee. A timer, or DOMContentLoaded, is a guess about load order rather than a guarantee: registration can land later, from an async chunk or a dynamically inserted script."}]}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ca9e799f-3e0b-44f3-8c23-c900a54d324e","uuid":"cd00bd1f-195d-432b-9f4d-b2502d6a7b0c","timestamp":"2026-08-25T12:17:06.362Z","tool_use_result":[{"type":"text","text":"## <kai-conversation-item>\n\n### Getting the element\nRegister it before you use it.
