If you skip registering it the element never upgrades: nothing renders, **no error and no warning is logged**, a property you set assigns and reads back correctly, and `customElements.whenDefined('kai-feedback-bar')` never resolves.\n\n```ts\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';\nimport '@kitn.ai/ui/elements/feedback-bar'; // or just this one\n```\n\nTypeScript: `import type { KaiFeedbackBarElement } from '@kitn.ai/ui/elements';` — the element interface ships with the package; do not hand-roll a structural type.\n\n### AI/UI contract\n`kai-*` elements accept **array and object data as JavaScript properties** (set in JavaScript via `el.property = value`, not as HTML attributes). Events are native CustomEvents — listen with `el.addEventListener(\"event-name\", handler)` and read `event.detail` for the payload.\n\n### Props (JavaScript properties)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **barTitle** `undefined | string`\n  The banner label (e.g. \"Was this helpful?\"). Attribute: `bar-title` (`title` is avoided because it is a global HTML attribute).\n- **collectDetail** `undefined | false | true`\n  When set, a not-helpful vote opens an optional detail form before the thank-you confirmation. Attribute: `collect-detail`.\n- **categories** `undefined | string[]` ⚑ set as a JS property, not an HTML attribute\n  Optional category chips for the detail form. Set as a JS property (array).\n- **detailTitle** `undefined | string`\n  Heading for the detail form. Attribute: `detail-title`.\n- **detailPlaceholder** `undefined | string`\n  Placeholder for the detail comment box. Attribute: `detail-placeholder`.\n- **submitLabel** `undefined | string`\n  Submit button label in the detail form. Attribute: `submit-label`.\n- **thanksMessage** `undefined | string`\n  Confirmation copy shown after a vote/submit. Attribute: `thanks-message`.\n\n### Attributes (HTML-safe)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **bar-title** `undefined | string`\n  The banner label (e.g. \"Was this helpful?\"). Attribute: `bar-title` (`title` is avoided because it is a global HTML attribute).\n- **collect-detail** `undefined | false | true`\n  When set, a not-helpful vote opens an optional detail form before the thank-you confirmation. Attribute: `collect-detail`.\n- **detail-title** `undefined | string`\n  Heading for the detail form. Attribute: `detail-title`.\n- **detail-placeholder** `undefined | string`\n  Placeholder for the detail comment box. Attribute: `detail-placeholder`.\n- **submit-label** `undefined | string`\n  Submit button label in the detail form. Attribute: `submit-label`.\n- **thanks-message** `undefined | string`\n  Confirmation copy shown after a vote/submit. Attribute: `thanks-message`.\n\n### Events (CustomEvent, listen via addEventListener)\n- **kai-close** — The user dismissed the banner.\n- **kai-feedback** — The user rated the response. `value` is `'helpful'` or `'not-helpful'`. `detail`: `{ value: \"helpful\" | \"not-helpful\" }`\n- **kai-feedback-detail** — The user submitted the optional detail form (`collect-detail`). `detail`: `{ value: \"helpful\" | \"not-helpful\"; category?: undefined | string; comment?: undefined | string }`\n\n### Invariants\nRules that have already broken real consumers of this kit. Each block says what enforces it — read that line rather than assuming CI catches a violation, because nothing here reads YOUR code. 3 of the 6 below are enforced by NOTHING at all, and 2 more by only half of what they say. Diagnosis and wrong/right examples for every invariant are served once, not repeated per element — call component_reference with { name: \"invariants\" }.\n\n#### props-not-attributes — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nSet arrays, objects and functions as JS PROPERTIES on the element instance. Only scalars (strings, numbers, booleans) belong in attributes, and the derived layer's scalar flag records which prop is which. What actually goes wrong, because the mechanism is not the obvious one: a framework template binding or String() stringifies an array to '[object Object]', which is not JSON, and the attribute path falls back to handing the element that raw STRING — so the prop is silently a string and the list renders nothing. A function cannot survive JSON at all: JSON.stringify({ onSubmit }) is '{}', so every callback is dropped. And an attribute re-set is not how updates are delivered — see reactivity-two-halves. A hand-written, valid-JSON attribute does happen to parse today, because a transitive dependency JSON.parses attributes whose declared default is not a string, but that is that dependency's behaviour and not this kit's contract; do not build on it. NOTHING IN THIS REPO ENFORCES THIS — it is a consumer contract, and the scalar flag in the derived layer is how the catalog SERVES the fact, which is not the same as checking it.\n\n#### events-non-bubbling — enforced by a structural guarantee in packages/ui/src/elements/define.tsx\nNon-bubbling is the default: public kai-* events are dispatched through the one helper that hard-codes bubbles:false and composed:false, so listen on the element itself, never on a parent or document. The protocol exceptions (kai-maximize-intent, kai-maximize-state and kai-card) bubble or compose deliberately and are listed in the derived layer under eventExceptions — do not generalise from them to the rest.\n\n#### host-coordinates — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nThere is no store. Data flows in via properties, out via events, and the host wires element A to element B. Solid context does not cross element boundaries, so nothing coordinates elements except the host application, and no element owns another. Placing two elements in the same subtree wires nothing.\n\n#### untrusted-model-output — PARTIALLY ENFORCED: one half is covered, by the kit's own tests (packages/ui/tests/components/markdown-xss.test.tsx, packages/ui/tests/components/artifact-url-xss.test.tsx, packages/ui/tests/components/hostile-model-output.test.tsx); the statement says which half is not\nEverything the model produced is untrusted input: a MessagePart, card envelope or tool argument reaching innerHTML, an href or src, window.open or an iframe is a vulnerability. THE DEFECT IS NEVER A MISSING GUARD, IT IS WHICH PATH GOT IT — every one found so far sat on a path the CONSUMER controls while the model-controlled path beside it had none. So put a policy on the sink, and MATCH THE LIST TO THE SINK rather than reaching for one universal list: http:, https: and mailto: for anything navigable the user may click, resolved against the page so ordinary relative links still work; http: and https: ONLY for a model-supplied citation, which is a reference to a page on the public web and has no business being relative. Those are two lists because there are two sinks — it is the same split the kit makes internally between SAFE_SCHEMES and RENDERABLE_SCHEMES, not a variant invented here. Whichever you use, parse inside a try/catch and RETURN FALSE on an unparseable URL: new URL() throws, and a throw at a sink crashes the render. And render model text as TEXT. Escaping is the correct rendering: the source text must stay VISIBLE as well as inert, because a filter that deleted it would pass the security check and be a worse UI. COVERAGE, and read this before trusting CI here: the three XSS suites are tests and ONLY tests. They run in the required test job, so the vectors they pin cannot come back — but NOTHING structural stops a NEW sink landing unguarded. No lint script in the package is about sinks, and the coupling map's unenforced list has no entry for the class. A new sink is caught in review or not at all.\n\n#### kit-parses-consumer-fetches — PARTIALLY ENFORCED: one half is covered, by the kit's `lint:silent-drops` script, in required CI; the statement says which half is not\nThe kit parses; the consumer fetches. Two halves, covered differently. KIT SIDE: every MessagePart variant the wire encodes must be accounted for, or a variant is gone once the request leaves — that half is enforced by lint:silent-drops in CI. CONSUMER SIDE: never hand-roll an SSE reader; import readOpenAIStream, readAnthropicStream or readModelStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire, and fetch from your own endpoint, because there is no client, no key handling and no provider SDK below wire/. NO CI CHECK COVERS THAT SECOND HALF — no guard reads consumer or scaffolded code for a hand-rolled reader; it is measured by the acceptance deck instead, at scenario S2, whose scoring line is 'imports readOpenAIStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire; no hand-rolled SSE reader anywhere in the output'.\n\n#### upgrade-race (script-tag delivery only) — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this, until issue #99 option B lands in defineWebComponent. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nA property set before the element upgrades is lost. On script-tag targets, load order is not ours. Until issue #99 option B (upgrade-property preservation in defineWebComponent) lands, every script-tag recipe must state this race loudly and set properties only after registration — await customElements.whenDefined(tag), which is the guarantee. A timer, or DOMContentLoaded, is a guess about load order rather than a guarantee: registration can land later, from an async chunk or a dynamically inserted script."}]}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ca9e799f-3e0b-44f3-8c23-c900a54d324e","uuid":"2a16af83-9124-480e-b87f-ae407d3edad3","timestamp":"2026-08-25T12:17:12.948Z","tool_use_result":[{"type":"text","text":"## <kai-feedback-bar>\n\n### Getting the element\nRegister it before you use it. Events are native CustomEvents — listen with `el.addEventListener(\"event-name\", handler)` and read `event.detail` for the payload.\n\n### Props (JavaScript properties)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **toasts** `undefined | { id: string; message: string; variant?: undefined | \"neutral\" | \"success\" | \"warning\" | \"error\" | \"info\"; appearance?: undefined | \"pill\" | \"card\"; inverse?: undefined | false | true; description?: undefined | string; action?: undefined | { label: string; onAction: () => void | false }; duration?: undefined | number; dismissible?: undefined | false | true; target?: undefined | HTMLElement }[]` ⚑ set as a JS property, not an HTML attribute\n  The toasts to render. Newest is shown on top. Set as a JS property (array); pass a new array reference to update. Omit for an empty region, which is the normal resting state and how the imperative `toast()` API starts.\n- **position** `undefined | \"top-center\" | \"top-right\" | \"top-left\" | \"bottom-center\" | \"bottom-right\" | \"bottom-left\"`\n  Stack anchor: `'top-center'` (default), `'top-right'`, `'bottom-center'`, …\n- **max** `undefined | number`\n  Max simultaneously-visible toasts; the rest queue. Defaults to `3`.\n- **stack** `undefined | \"expanded\" | \"collapsed\"`\n  Stacking: 'expanded' (default, full column) | 'collapsed' (Sonner-style pile that expands on hover/focus). Attribute: stack.\n- **appearance** `undefined | \"pill\" | \"card\"`\n  Default appearance for this region's toasts: `'pill'` (default, compact) | `'card'` (richer, with a description line). A per-toast `appearance` wins. Attribute: `appearance`.\n- **inverse** `undefined | false | true`\n  Default high-contrast inverse treatment for this region's toasts. A per-toast `inverse` wins. Off by default. Attribute: `inverse`.\n- **target** `undefined | HTMLElement`\n  Container element to anchor this region to (JS property). Set by the store for a scoped region; unset = the global viewport region.\n\n### Attributes (HTML-safe)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **position** `undefined | \"top-center\" | \"top-right\" | \"top-left\" | \"bottom-center\" | \"bottom-right\" | \"bottom-left\"`\n  Stack anchor: `'top-center'` (default), `'top-right'`, `'bottom-center'`, …\n- **max** `undefined | number`\n  Max simultaneously-visible toasts; the rest queue. Attribute: `inverse`.\n\n### Events (CustomEvent, listen via addEventListener)\n- **kai-action** — A toast's action button was pressed. `detail`: `{ id: string; label: string }`\n- **kai-dismiss** — A toast left the stack. `reason` is `'timeout' | 'close' | 'action'`. `detail`: `{ id: string; reason: \"timeout\" | \"close\" | \"action\" }`\n\n### Invariants\nRules that have already broken real consumers of this kit. Each block says what enforces it — read that line rather than assuming CI catches a violation, because nothing here reads YOUR code. 3 of the 6 below are enforced by NOTHING at all, and 2 more by only half of what they say. Diagnosis and wrong/right examples for every invariant are served once, not repeated per element — call component_reference with { name: \"invariants\" }.\n\n#### props-not-attributes — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nSet arrays, objects and functions as JS PROPERTIES on the element instance. Only scalars (strings, numbers, booleans) belong in attributes, and the derived layer's scalar flag records which prop is which. What actually goes wrong, because the mechanism is not the obvious one: a framework template binding or String() stringifies an array to '[object Object]', which is not JSON, and the attribute path falls back to handing the element that raw STRING — so the prop is silently a string and the list renders nothing. A function cannot survive JSON at all: JSON.stringify({ onSubmit }) is '{}', so every callback is dropped. And an attribute re-set is not how updates are delivered — see reactivity-two-halves. A hand-written, valid-JSON attribute does happen to parse today, because a transitive dependency JSON.parses attributes whose declared default is not a string, but that is that dependency's behaviour and not this kit's contract; do not build on it. NOTHING IN THIS REPO ENFORCES THIS — it is a consumer contract, and the scalar flag in the derived layer is how the catalog SERVES the fact, which is not the same as checking it.\n\n#### events-non-bubbling — enforced by a structural guarantee in packages/ui/src/elements/define.tsx\nNon-bubbling is the default: public kai-* events are dispatched through the one helper that hard-codes bubbles:false and composed:false, so listen on the element itself, never on a parent or document. The protocol exceptions (kai-maximize-intent, kai-maximize-state and kai-card) bubble or compose deliberately and are listed in the derived layer under eventExceptions — do not generalise from them to the rest.\n\n#### host-coordinates — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nThere is no store. Data flows in via properties, out via events, and the host wires element A to element B. Solid context does not cross element boundaries, so nothing coordinates elements except the host application, and no element owns another. Placing two elements in the same subtree wires nothing.\n\n#### untrusted-model-output — PARTIALLY ENFORCED: one half is covered, by the kit's own tests (packages/ui/tests/components/markdown-xss.test.tsx, packages/ui/tests/components/artifact-url-xss.test.tsx, packages/ui/tests/components/hostile-model-output.test.tsx); the statement says which half is not\nEverything the model produced is untrusted input: a MessagePart, card envelope or tool argument reaching innerHTML, an href or src, window.open or an iframe is a vulnerability. THE DEFECT IS NEVER A MISSING GUARD, IT IS WHICH PATH GOT IT — every one found so far sat on a path the CONSUMER controls while the model-controlled path beside it had none. So put a policy on the sink, and MATCH THE LIST TO THE SINK rather than reaching for one universal list: http:, https: and mailto: for anything navigable the user may click, resolved against the page so ordinary relative links still work; http: and https: ONLY for a model-supplied citation, which is a reference to a page on the public web and has no business being relative. Those are two lists because there are two sinks — it is the same split the kit makes internally between SAFE_SCHEMES and RENDERABLE_SCHEMES, not a variant invented here. Whichever you use, parse inside a try/catch and RETURN FALSE on an unparseable URL: new URL() throws, and a throw at a sink crashes the render. And render model text as TEXT. Escaping is the correct rendering: the source text must stay VISIBLE as well as inert, because a filter that deleted it would pass the security check and be a worse UI. COVERAGE, and read this before trusting CI here: the three XSS suites are tests and ONLY tests. They run in the required test job, so the vectors they pin cannot come back — but NOTHING structural stops a NEW sink landing unguarded. No lint script in the package is about sinks, and the coupling map's unenforced list has no entry for the class. A new sink is caught in review or not at all.\n\n#### kit-parses-consumer-fetches — PARTIALLY ENFORCED: one half is covered, by the kit's `lint:silent-drops` script, in required CI; the statement says which half is not\nThe kit parses; the consumer fetches. Two halves, covered differently. KIT SIDE: every MessagePart variant the wire encodes must be accounted for, or a variant is gone once the request leaves — that half is enforced by lint:silent-drops in CI. CONSUMER SIDE: never hand-roll an SSE reader; import readOpenAIStream, readAnthropicStream or readModelStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire, and fetch from your own endpoint, because there is no client, no key handling and no provider SDK below wire/. NO CI CHECK COVERS THAT SECOND HALF — no guard reads consumer or scaffolded code for a hand-rolled reader; it is measured by the acceptance deck instead, at scenario S2, whose scoring line is 'imports readOpenAIStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire; no hand-rolled SSE reader anywhere in the output'.\n\n#### upgrade-race (script-tag delivery only) — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this, until issue #99 option B lands in defineWebComponent. Events are native CustomEvents — listen with `el.addEventListener(\"event-name\", handler)` and read `event.detail` for the payload.\n\n### Props (JavaScript properties)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **multiple** `undefined | false | true`\n  Allow selecting multiple files (default true).\n- **accept** `undefined | string`\n  `accept` attribute for the file picker (e.g. `image/*`).\n- **disabled** `undefined | false | true`\n  Disable the dropzone: no clicking, no drag-and-drop.\n- **label** `undefined | string`\n  Default dropzone label (overridable via the default slot).\n\n### Attributes (HTML-safe)\n- **theme** `\"light\" | \"dark\" | \"auto\"`\n  Color mode (`auto` follows prefers-color-scheme).\n- **multiple** `undefined | false | true`\n  Allow selecting multiple files (default true).\n- **accept** `undefined | string`\n  `accept` attribute for the file picker (e.g. `image/*`).\n- **disabled** `undefined | false | true`\n  Disable the dropzone: no clicking, no drag-and-drop.\n- **label** `undefined | string`\n  Default dropzone label (overridable via the default slot).\n\n### Events (CustomEvent, listen via addEventListener)\n- **kai-files-added** — Files were picked or dropped. `detail`: `{ files: File[] }`\n\n### Composition slots\nProject your own markup into these named regions — add a light-DOM child with a `slot=\"…\"` attribute (e.g. `<div slot=\"sidebar\">…</div>`).\n- **** — Custom dropzone content, replacing the default label (the `label` prop is the fallback).\n\n### Invariants\nRules that have already broken real consumers of this kit. Each block says what enforces it — read that line rather than assuming CI catches a violation, because nothing here reads YOUR code. 3 of the 6 below are enforced by NOTHING at all, and 2 more by only half of what they say. Diagnosis and wrong/right examples for every invariant are served once, not repeated per element — call component_reference with { name: \"invariants\" }.\n\n#### props-not-attributes — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nSet arrays, objects and functions as JS PROPERTIES on the element instance. Only scalars (strings, numbers, booleans) belong in attributes, and the derived layer's scalar flag records which prop is which. What actually goes wrong, because the mechanism is not the obvious one: a framework template binding or String() stringifies an array to '[object Object]', which is not JSON, and the attribute path falls back to handing the element that raw STRING — so the prop is silently a string and the list renders nothing. A function cannot survive JSON at all: JSON.stringify({ onSubmit }) is '{}', so every callback is dropped. And an attribute re-set is not how updates are delivered — see reactivity-two-halves. A hand-written, valid-JSON attribute does happen to parse today, because a transitive dependency JSON.parses attributes whose declared default is not a string, but that is that dependency's behaviour and not this kit's contract; do not build on it. NOTHING IN THIS REPO ENFORCES THIS — it is a consumer contract, and the scalar flag in the derived layer is how the catalog SERVES the fact, which is not the same as checking it.\n\n#### events-non-bubbling — enforced by a structural guarantee in packages/ui/src/elements/define.tsx\nNon-bubbling is the default: public kai-* events are dispatched through the one helper that hard-codes bubbles:false and composed:false, so listen on the element itself, never on a parent or document. The protocol exceptions (kai-maximize-intent, kai-maximize-state and kai-card) bubble or compose deliberately and are listed in the derived layer under eventExceptions — do not generalise from them to the rest.\n\n#### host-coordinates — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nThere is no store. Data flows in via properties, out via events, and the host wires element A to element B. Solid context does not cross element boundaries, so nothing coordinates elements except the host application, and no element owns another. Placing two elements in the same subtree wires nothing.\n\n#### untrusted-model-output — PARTIALLY ENFORCED: one half is covered, by the kit's own tests (packages/ui/tests/components/markdown-xss.test.tsx, packages/ui/tests/components/artifact-url-xss.test.tsx, packages/ui/tests/components/hostile-model-output.test.tsx); the statement says which half is not\nEverything the model produced is untrusted input: a MessagePart, card envelope or tool argument reaching innerHTML, an href or src, window.open or an iframe is a vulnerability. THE DEFECT IS NEVER A MISSING GUARD, IT IS WHICH PATH GOT IT — every one found so far sat on a path the CONSUMER controls while the model-controlled path beside it had none. So put a policy on the sink, and MATCH THE LIST TO THE SINK rather than reaching for one universal list: http:, https: and mailto: for anything navigable the user may click, resolved against the page so ordinary relative links still work; http: and https: ONLY for a model-supplied citation, which is a reference to a page on the public web and has no business being relative. Those are two lists because there are two sinks — it is the same split the kit makes internally between SAFE_SCHEMES and RENDERABLE_SCHEMES, not a variant invented here. Whichever you use, parse inside a try/catch and RETURN FALSE on an unparseable URL: new URL() throws, and a throw at a sink crashes the render. And render model text as TEXT. Escaping is the correct rendering: the source text must stay VISIBLE as well as inert, because a filter that deleted it would pass the security check and be a worse UI. COVERAGE, and read this before trusting CI here: the three XSS suites are tests and ONLY tests. They run in the required test job, so the vectors they pin cannot come back — but NOTHING structural stops a NEW sink landing unguarded. No lint script in the package is about sinks, and the coupling map's unenforced list has no entry for the class. A new sink is caught in review or not at all.\n\n#### kit-parses-consumer-fetches — PARTIALLY ENFORCED: one half is covered, by the kit's `lint:silent-drops` script, in required CI; the statement says which half is not\nThe kit parses; the consumer fetches. Two halves, covered differently. KIT SIDE: every MessagePart variant the wire encodes must be accounted for, or a variant is gone once the request leaves — that half is enforced by lint:silent-drops in CI. CONSUMER SIDE: never hand-roll an SSE reader; import readOpenAIStream, readAnthropicStream or readModelStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire, and fetch from your own endpoint, because there is no client, no key handling and no provider SDK below wire/. NO CI CHECK COVERS THAT SECOND HALF — no guard reads consumer or scaffolded code for a hand-rolled reader; it is measured by the acceptance deck instead, at scenario S2, whose scoring line is 'imports readOpenAIStream from @kitn.ai/ui/wire; no hand-rolled SSE reader anywhere in the output'.\n\n#### upgrade-race (script-tag delivery only) — NOT ENFORCED: nothing in the kit checks this, until issue #99 option B lands in defineWebComponent. A rule you apply, not a guarantee you will be warned about\nA property set before the element upgrades is lost. On script-tag targets, load order is not ours. Until issue #99 option B (upgrade-property preservation in defineWebComponent) lands, every script-tag recipe must state this race loudly and set properties only after registration — await customElements.whenDefined(tag), which is the guarantee. A timer, or DOMContentLoaded, is a guess about load order rather than a guarantee: registration can land later, from an async chunk or a dynamically inserted script."}]}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ca9e799f-3e0b-44f3-8c23-c900a54d324e","uuid":"ebb9ebe8-2485-43e3-a788-d12117b62358","timestamp":"2026-08-25T12:17:23.634Z","tool_use_result":[{"type":"text","text":"## <kai-file-upload>\n\n### Getting the element\nRegister it before you use it. A plain string, so unlike `messages` it DOES work as an attribute. Omitted means no filter. MEDIA TYPES ONLY -- exact (`image/png`) or subtype wildcard (`text/*`). HTML allows a file extension here and this does not: `accept=\".py\"` THROWS with the entry named, rather than silently resolving to a picker that accepts nothing. It can only NARROW what the kit can already encode: `accept=\"image/*\"` resolves to the four image formats both APIs take, not to every image type the OS offers. Pass the SAME string to `toOpenAIMessages(msgs, { accept })` and the picker and the wire cannot disagree -- both resolve it through `resolveMediaPolicy` against one declaration. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01SSgsf6NZEL8GfjezR76mRJ","type":"tool_result","content":"import { ChatMessage, MessagePart, Source } from '../elements/chat-types.js';\nimport { ToolPart } from '../components/tool-types.js';\nimport { CardEnvelope } from '../primitives/card-contract.js';\nimport { AttachmentData } from '../components/attachment-types.js';\nimport { ReasoningOpts } from './parts.js';\n/** The one universal contract: a functional-updater setter (React setState shape). */\nexport type SetMessages = (updater: (prev: ChatMessage[]) => ChatMessage[]) => void;\n/** Every OBJECT payload a `MessagePart` variant carries. `type`/`raw` are the\n *  variant's own bookkeeping, not payload; the primitive payloads (`text`,\n *  `label`, `index`, ...) drop out at `Extract<..., object>`. */\ntype PartPayload = Extract<MessagePart extends infer P ? (P extends object ? P[Exclude<keyof P, 'type' | 'raw'>] : never) : never, object>;\n/** Every bag one of these mutators takes: the part payloads plus the options\n *  bags that are not payloads themselves. */\ntype MutatorBag = PartPayload | ReasoningOpts;\n/** `keyof` over a union member-by-member. The bare `keyof (A | B)` is the\n *  INTERSECTION of their keys, which is the opposite of what this needs. */\ntype KeysOf<T> = T extends unknown ? keyof T : never;\n/** `Shape`, but any key that belongs exclusively to a SIBLING bag is a compile\n *  error. Keys `Shape` never heard of are untouched, so a consumer's own\n *  superset of a citation still passes — only the mix-ups fail. That is a\n *  denylist, not an exact type, and deliberately so: boundary point 1. */\ntype Unmixed<Shape> = Shape & {\n    [K in Exclude<KeysOf<MutatorBag>, keyof Shape>]?: never;\n};\n/** A fluent builder for one in-flight assistant message. Owns no state. */\nexport interface AssistantStream {\n    readonly id: string;\n    appendText(delta: string): AssistantStream;\n    appendReasoning(delta: string, opts?: Unmixed<ReasoningOpts>): AssistantStream;\n    upsertTool(toolCallId: string, patch: Unmixed<Partial<ToolPart>>): AssistantStream;\n    /** Adds a card, or REPLACES the existing one with the same `envelope.id`. A\n     *  model that revises a card mid-turn re-sends the whole envelope, so a second\n     *  call with a known id revises that card in place rather than rendering a\n     *  second copy of it. See `upsertCardPart`. */\n    addCard(envelope: CardEnvelope): AssistantStream;\n    addSource(source: Unmixed<Source>): AssistantStream;\n    addFile(attachment: AttachmentData): AssistantStream;\n    done(): void;\n    /** Settles the turn as FAILED and puts `reason` where the reader can find it.\n     *\n     *  WHERE THE REASON LANDS, in order:\n     *  1. every tool part that has NOT produced a result flips to `output-error`\n     *     with `errorText: reason`, so no panel spins forever. A tool ALREADY in\n     *     `output-error` with its own non-empty `errorText` is left exactly as it\n     *     is: \"search index offline\" is the answer to what went wrong and\n     *     \"Connection lost.\" is the generic outer symptom, so overwriting the\n     *     specific with the generic loses the only actionable thing on the\n     *     message. It still counts as carrying the failure. A tool in\n     *     `output-error` with NO text does get filled in — an error panel with\n     *     nothing in it says no more than a blank bubble does.\n     *  2. if no part was able to carry it, the reason is APPENDED as its own text\n     *     part.\n     *\n     *  Never both — a turn does not report the same failure twice.\n     *\n     *  \"Where the reader can FIND it\" is deliberate, and rule 1 and rule 2 are not\n     *  equally loud. Rule 2 is text in the thread: read without any interaction.\n     *  Rule 1 is a tool panel, which renders COLLAPSED — the header shows an error\n     *  icon and a badge, so the failure is unmissable, but the `errorText` itself\n     *  is one click away. That is the right default for a failed tool inside an\n     *  otherwise readable answer, and it is why rule 2 exists rather than always\n     *  stamping the panel and calling it reported.\n     *\n     *  Rule 2 is the whole point and it used to be missing. `abort` only ever did\n     *  rule 1, so a TEXT-ONLY turn — every turn of a text-only support widget —\n     *  had nothing to stamp: the string was discarded and a failed request\n     *  rendered an EMPTY assistant bubble, while the consumer that passed a\n     *  perfectly good sentence believed it had reported the failure. That is the\n     *  repo's \"decide loudly\" rule broken on the one path where being quiet is\n     *  worst, and it shipped because the discard is invisible from the call site.\n     *\n     *  It is a NEW part, never a merge onto the trailing text: gluing \"Connection\n     *  lost.\" onto the model's half-finished sentence reads as the model saying\n     *  it. How does a user attach a file *in `kai-composer`*?\n`component_reference` for `kai-composer` lists no attachment prop, no attach\nbutton, no `kai-attachments-change` event, and no file affordance of any kind.\n`kai-prompt-input` and `kai-chat` do have `attach` / `attachments` /\n`kai-attachments-change`, and their `kai-submit` detail carries `attachments` —\n`kai-composer`'s does not. `debug` matched no pattern for the question.\n\n**Guess:** the picker is the host's. A paperclip button + hidden\n`<input type=\"file\">` sit in the composer row, and a `kai-attachments`\nelement (`variant=\"inline\" removable`) is the staging tray above the composer.\nOn `kai-submit` the staged items become `file` parts on the user message.\nIf the kit intends `kai-prompt-input` to be the only composer with attachments,\nthis app diverges from that intent because the brief named `kai-composer`.\n\n### 2. What belongs in `AttachmentData.url` for a locally-picked `File`?\nThe docs give the shape (`{ id, type, filename, mediaType, url, title }`) but\nnever say what `url` should be for a file the user just picked — a `blob:`\nobject URL, a data URL, or a URL you get back after uploading first.\n\n**Guess:** `URL.createObjectURL(file)`.\n\n### 3. What is the object-URL lifecycle policy?\nThe package's own `package.json` has a `lint:attachment-object-urls` script, so\nthere clearly *is* a policy, but no allowed doc states it — nothing says who\nrevokes, or when.\n\n**Guess:** revoke when a staged attachment is removed before sending, and on\n`beforeunload`; never revoke one the thread still renders, which would blank\nthe preview.\n\n### 4. How is a `kai-conversation-item` selected?\n`component_reference` documents `conversationId` / `active` / `compact` and\nsays the row \"hands its identity to the container's selection contract\" — but\nlists **no events at all** on the element. `kai-conversation-select` is\ndocumented on `kai-conversations` (the container), which this app does not use.\n\n**Guess:** a plain `click` listener on each `<kai-conversation-item>` host, with\nthe host setting `active` on the rows. Consequence I could not resolve: the\nroving-tabindex / arrow-key navigation the docs mention is presumably driven by\nthe container, so keyboard traversal across a hand-rolled list of items may not\nbehave the way `kai-conversations` would.\n\n### 5. `createMockResponder` is essentially undocumented in the allowed docs.\nIt appears **only** in the MCP `scaffold` output (as\n`const mockResponse = createMockResponder()`). It is absent from the README,\nfrom `llms.txt` / `llms-full.txt`, and the MCP `debug` tool matched nothing for\na direct question about its signature, options, or tool-call support.\n\n**Where I had to go outside the allowed docs:** to learn its options\n(`replies`, `delayMs`, `chunkSize`, `announce`) and that a reply may be a\n`MockTurn` with `toolCalls`, I read the *shipped type declarations* in\n`node_modules/@kitn.ai/ui/dist/state/mock.d.ts`. That is the installed\npackage's `.d.ts`, not npm/GitHub source, but it is still outside the three\nsanctioned doc surfaces — flagging it explicitly.\n\nSame applies to two more shapes no allowed doc covered:\n- `AssistantStream`'s methods (`upsertTool`, `abort`, `done`) —\n  `dist/state/stream.d.ts`.\n- `ModelTurn` (what `readOpenAIStream` resolves with: `toolCalls[].id`,\n  `.input`, `.argumentsText`, `.error`) — `dist/wire/chunk.d.ts`.\n\n### 6. Do the *default* mock replies ever emit a tool call?\n`DEFAULT_MOCK_REPLIES` is exported but its contents are not documented.\n\n**Guess:** rather than depend on it, `src/main.ts` passes its own `replies`\narray with an explicit `{ text, toolCalls: [...] }` turn, so a tool call is\nguaranteed to appear on the third turn.\n\n### 7. Who resolves a tool call the mock announces?\nThe mock frames a tool call the way a provider does — announce + streamed\narguments — which leaves the tool part in `input-available`. Nothing in the\ndocs says whether the kit, the mock, or the host is supposed to supply the\nresult, so an unanswered call would spin in the thread forever.\n\n**Guess:** the host does it. After `readOpenAIStream` resolves, `main.ts` walks\n`result.toolCalls` and calls `stream.upsertTool(id, { state: 'output-available',\noutput: {...} })` with a stub result — the same seam a real app would put its\ntool result in.\n\n### 8. How do you drive a hand-placed `<kai-toast-region>`?\n`component_reference` documents the `toasts` property and the `kai-dismiss` /\n`kai-action` events, and the scaffold's interaction patterns document the\n*imperative* `toast()`, which auto-mounts its own region on `document.body`.\nNothing says how the two relate, or whether calling `toast()` while your own\nregion is on the page produces one region or two.\n\n**Guess:** avoid `toast()` entirely. This app places one `<kai-toast-region>`,\nowns the `toasts` array, appends to it, and prunes on `kai-dismiss`.\n\n### 9. Where does the composer go relative to `kai-thread`?\n`kai-thread`'s only slot is `empty`; there is no composer slot, and no recipe\ncovers a hand-composed thread (`recipes` returns only `workspace-chat` and\n`support-widget-script-tag`, both built on `kai-chat`).\n\n**Guess:** the composer is a flex sibling below the thread, with the thread\ntaking `flex: 1; min-height: 0` so its internal scroll area works.\n\n### 10. `kai-composer` has no send button.\nOnly `kai-prompt-input` documents a `submit` prop and a `::part(send)`. The\ncomposer exposes `send()` as a method and submits on Enter.\n\n**Guess:** an app-owned \"Send\" button that calls `composer.send()`.\n\n### 11. Placement / lifecycle of `kai-feedback-bar`.\nNothing documents whether it is meant to be per-message or per-thread, or when\nit should appear and disappear.\n\n**Guess:** one thread-level bar, hidden by default, revealed after each\nassistant turn settles, re-hidden on the next submit or on `kai-close`.\n\n### 12. `theme.css` vs `theme.tokens.css`.\nThe README says to import `@kitn.ai/ui/theme.css`; the MCP scaffold imports\n`@kitn.ai/ui/theme.tokens.css` and comments \"use `theme.css` only for\nTailwind-source apps\". The two are not reconciled anywhere.\n\n**Guess:** followed the scaffold — `theme.tokens.css`, since this app has no\nTailwind build.\n\n### 13. Styling the shadow-DOM internals of a hand-composed surface.\n`::part()` names are documented per element, but there is no guidance on the\nhost-side layout contract (does `kai-composer` size itself? does `kai-thread`\nneed an explicit height?).\n\n**Guess:** `display: block` + explicit flex sizing on each element from\n`src/styles.css`, and a wrapper `z-index: 90` so it stays under the toast\nregion's `var(--kai-toast-z, 100)` — that last number *is* documented, in the\nscaffold's full-page comment block.\nMD\necho written","description":"Write NOTES.md, clean up"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1565,"cache_read_input_tokens":132566,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1565},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ca9e799f-3e0b-44f3-8c23-c900a54d324e","uuid":"091ee5a3-3ba5-4ea9-b81e-5ddb342c45a9","timestamp":"2026-08-25T12:22:29.532Z","request_id":"req_011CePTVimWNCDSHeBiUXvQr"}
